<div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-91"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="91" data-folio="91" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n90_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 91">[p. 91]</button></div><h2 class="sec-h1">The</h2><h2 class="sec-h1">Register of Letters &amp;c.</h2><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-93"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="93" data-folio="1" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n92_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 1">[p. 1]</button></div><h2 class="sec-h1">THE</h2><h2 class="sec-h1">REGISTER OF LETTERS, &amp;c.</h2><h3 class="sec-h2"><span class="sc">of the</span></h3><h3 class="sec-h2"><span class="sc">Governor &amp; Company of Merchants of London</span></h3><h3 class="sec-h2"><em>TRADING INTO THE EAST INDIES.</em></h3><figure class="archival-plate" id="fig-007"><img src="/figures/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/fig-007_p0093.webp" alt="Decorative headpiece depicting foliage, flowers, and a fantastical curled animal/dragon at center." loading="lazy"></figure><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">An order taken by the lles: for such as haue promised &amp; sett downe their Contribucons &amp; not brought the said contribucons to the Trer.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">VPPON<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-001" href="#sec-04-fn-001" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-001" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> complainte made the łłe by [the] new authorized Companie of m<sup>r</sup>chauntę preparinge to trade to the East Indies that certen p̱sons w<sup>ch</sup> heeretofore p̱mysed and assured by their subscripcõns when the saide voyadge was first proiected to advanc[e] certen somes of money for the p̱ceedinge in the same vpon confidence &amp; assurance whereof the p<sup>r</sup>paracõn for the said voyadge hath been vndertaken and advanced to a full redines, and yet neverthelesse that the said p̱sons would since vpon some vndue p<sup>r</sup>tentes retract their former p̱myses in that behalfe Whereof would ensue y<sup>t</sup> by the want of their p̱mysed contribucons the voyadge must breake. Their Lo<sup>ps</sup>: vppon due Consideracõn <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-94" data-page="94" data-folio="2" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n93_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 2">[p. 2]</button> how greatelie it doth importe y<sup>e</sup> hono<sup>r</sup> of the realme and thestate of the present affaires thereof that the said voyadge should not be hindred or ou<sup>r</sup>throwne by any such default and indirect meanes, and Considering alsoe how weake allegãcõns of excuse haue been made by the p̄sons refusers it hath been therefore this day ordered by their Lo<sup>ps</sup> that notw<sup>th</sup>- standinge the p<sup>r</sup>tences of the foresaid p̄sons they shall w<sup>th</sup>out further delaie and namelie by Saterday next furnish their pm̃ysed Contribucõns or otherwyse that order shalbe taken by the łłe w<sup>th</sup> such as shall refuse the same to Committ them t[o] prison vntill they Conforme themselues &amp; make satisfaccõn accordinge to their subscripcõns. the xj<sup>th</sup> of Ianuarie 1600.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Capt Lancaster his Comission genrall of the ffleete to the East Indies from the Queene.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Elizabeth</span> by the grace of God Queene of England &amp;c To o<sup>r</sup> trustie and welbeloued Iames Lancaster<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-002" href="#sec-04-fn-002" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-002" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Esqier Greeting. Whereas diuers of o<sup>r</sup> loueing subiectę haue been peticioners vnto vs for o<sup>r</sup> Royall assent to be graunted vnto them that they att their owne adventures Costs and chardges aswell for the hono<sup>r</sup> of this o<sup>r</sup> Realme of England, as for the increase &amp; advancem<sup>te</sup> of Trade and m<sup>r</sup>chandize w<sup>th</sup>in the same, might Adventure and sett forth certeine voyages to the East Indies w<sup>th</sup> a Convenient nomber of shipps &amp; pinnaces by way of traffique and m<sup>r</sup>chandizeinge: Wee gratiouslie <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-95" data-page="95" data-folio="3" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n94_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 3">[p. 3]</button> inclininge to their humble petic̃on in that behalfe and favoringe soe good a desier &amp; honorable enterprize. Haue been pleased to giue lycence vnto o<sup>r</sup> said Subiecte to pceede in the said voiadge, &amp; for the better inabling them to establish a trade into &amp; from the said East Indies Haue by o<sup>r</sup> łres Pattente vnder o<sup>r</sup> greate seale of England beareing date at Westminster the last daie of december last past incorporated o<sup>r</sup> said Subiecte by the name of the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Companie of the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London trading into the East Indies, &amp; in the same łres Pattente haue geven them the sole trade of theast Indies for the terme of XV<sup>teen</sup> yeares w<sup>th</sup> diuers priuiledges &amp; ymunnities mencioned in the said łres Pattente, as therein y<sup>t</sup> doth &amp; may appeare. And whereas by vertue of o<sup>r</sup> said lycence &amp; łres Pattente soe by vs graunted to them, they haue prepared &amp; furnished seu<sup>r</sup>all shipps w<sup>th</sup> a Convenient nomber of m<sup>r</sup>chaunte Mariners and other o<sup>r</sup> said subiecte to be vsed and ymployed in the said voyadge &amp; haue chosen you to be the principall Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> or generall of all the said m<sup>r</sup>chaunte Mariners and other o<sup>r</sup> said Subiecte w<sup>ch</sup> are or shall be shipped in any of the said shipps. Wee gratiouslie favoring the said enterprize &amp; approueing &amp; shallowinge of their choise of yo<sup>u</sup> to the said Gou<sup>r</sup>nmente, being desirous to furnish you w<sup>th</sup> all fitt and Convenient power &amp; authoritie to rule and Gou<sup>r</sup>ne all and everie o<sup>r</sup> subiects ymployed in this voyadge by a due obedience to be by them yealded unto yo<sup>u</sup>, in the observing &amp; executing of all good orders &amp; constitucõns as you shall thinke Convenient to ordaine &amp; appointe for the furtheraunce of the said voiadge, to the ho: of vs and o<sup>r</sup> Realme &amp; for the advauncment of the said trade we doe hereby straightlie chardge and Comaund all &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y pson and psons ymployed vsed or shipped or w<sup>ch</sup> shall be ymployed vsed or shipped in this voiadge in the said ffower shipps, or any of them, to giue all due obedience &amp; respect vnto yo<sup>u</sup>, during the said voiadge, &amp; to beare themselues one towardes another in all good order and quietnes for any occasion that should breed mutinie, quarrells or discention amongst them to the hindraunce of the good successe w<sup>ch</sup> is to be hoped for through Gods providence of the said intended voyadge &amp; in default of such dutie &amp; obedience to be pformed towardes you, &amp; for the Correcc̃on &amp; quenchinge of all such mutenie quarrells or dissencõns that shall or may <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-96" data-page="96" data-folio="4" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n95_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 4">[p. 4]</button> growe or be mooued by the disorder evill disposic̃on or pversnes of any of the said psons. Wee doe heareby authorize you, to Chastice Correct &amp; ponish all offenders, &amp; transgressor<sup>rs</sup> in that behalfe according to the quallitie of their offence, w<sup>th</sup> such ponishmente as are comonlie vsed in all o<sup>r</sup> Armes by Sea when the offences are not Capitall, &amp; for capitall Offences as wilfull murder w<sup>ch</sup> is hatefull in the sight of God, or any notable muteny w<sup>ch</sup> is one offence that may tend to the ouerthrowe of the said voyadge the same beinge trulie and iustlie proued against any the pson or psons aforesaid. Wee doe hereby giue you full power and aucthoritie to vse &amp; putt in execuc̃on o<sup>r</sup> law called Marshall lawe in that behalfe. And theis o<sup>r</sup> łres shall be therein yo<sup>r</sup> sufficient warrant &amp; dischardge for the doing and Executing of all &amp; singuler the premisses. In witnes whereof we haue caused o<sup>r</sup> łres to be made Patente, Witnes o<sup>r</sup> selfe at Westminster the xxiiij<sup>th</sup> of Ianuarie in the xliij<sup>th</sup> yeare of o<sup>r</sup> Reigne.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-003" href="#sec-04-fn-003" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-003" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Comission to Capteine Lancaster Gournor and in succession to others.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Wheareas</span><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-004" href="#sec-04-fn-004" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-004" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> we the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie of the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London tradinge into the East Indies haue chosen you m<sup>r</sup> Lancaster for the cheefe Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> or gen<sup>r</sup>all to gouerne or rule all such marchaunte, Mariners, officers &amp; other her Ma<sup>ties</sup> subiecte w<sup>ch</sup> are ymployed by vs or are or shall be shipped in any of the ffower shipps by vs prepared and set forth for this present intended voiadge towardes the East Indies And whereas the Queenes most excellent ma<sup>ty</sup> approueing and allowinge of o<sup>r</sup> choise of you to the said Gou<sup>r</sup>nment and favo<sup>r</sup>ing the said enterprize hath by her gratious łres Patente vnder the greate seale of England the better to inable yo<sup>u</sup> to keepe yo<sup>r</sup> wholle Companie in good agrement one towardes another and in obedience and due respect towardes you, geaven you authoritie to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-97" data-page="97" data-folio="5" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n96_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 5">[p. 5]</button> chastice and correct such offences as shall aryse in the said voiadge accordinge as in the said lres Patente att lardge appeareth w<sup>ch</sup> lres Patente extendinge onelie to the generall gouernm<sup>te</sup> of yo<sup>r</sup> wholle Companie shipped in the said shipps, for their Civill behavio<sup>r</sup> whilest the are abroade in the said voiadge and not unto the managinge &amp; ordering of the trade of m<sup>r</sup>chandize for w<sup>ch</sup> the voyadge is principallie appointed and sett forth. Wee therfore by theis o<sup>r</sup> present lres or Comission under o<sup>r</sup> comon seale for the orderinge and disposeinge of all such m<sup>r</sup>chandize, gould, pearle Iewells and other Comodities w<sup>ch</sup> are to be bought bartered procured exchandged or otherwyse obteyned in this present voyadge doe for yo<sup>r</sup> direcc̃on and remembrance in that behalfe sett downe theis clawses ordenances and decrees heerafter followinge: declaringe heerby o<sup>r</sup> purpose and intenc̃on how we appointe and ordeine that the traffique of this present voiadge shall be ordered and caried: w<sup>ch</sup> ordenances and decrees wee will and requier you to observe and keepe, and doe giue yo<sup>u</sup> power and aucthoritie to see the same executed and putt in vse accordinglie viz</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">We doe ordeine and decree that all the preparac̃ons of moneys m<sup>r</sup>chandizes and other provision for the said voyadge and all Comodities moneys Iewells and other marchandize retourned in the said voiadge shall be holden reputed and accompted and be carried managed ordered and handled as one entire Accompt and comen stocke of adventure wherein noe private traffique barter exchandge or m<sup>r</sup>chandizeing shall be vsed practized or admitted by any pticuler Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> Capten marchaunte Agent ffacto<sup>r</sup> m<sup>r</sup> Marriner officer or other pson whatsoever imployed in the said voiadge or pmitted to goe in the same upon peine of the losse &amp; forfecture to the vse of the generall Companie and adventurers in this voyadge of all somes of moneys Iewells wares goodes or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes w<sup>ch</sup> shall be found in the said shipps or ells were carried forth or retourned home by any private or pticuler man and not conteyned and brought into the generall and Comon accompte &amp; ioynt adventure of the said voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> to thend this preiudice of private traffique may the better be avoided We doe ordeine and appointe that due Inquisic̃on be made in all and euery y<sup>e</sup> severall shipps of the said voyadge and elswheare by search of all such Cheste, Boxes, <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-98" data-page="98" data-folio="6" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n97_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 6">[p. 6]</button> Packe, Packette, Bookes, writinge and other meanes whereby discouery may be made of the breach of this present ordenance.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> we doe in like manner ordeine and decree for the avoideinge of all vnfaithfullnes, and deceipte to be vsed in the said voyadge in the defraudinge of the gen<sup>r</sup>all adventure w<sup>ch</sup> is p<sup>r</sup>pared and set forth att the greate Coste and chardge of such as repose their trust and confidence in the officers and mynistors imployed in the said voyadge that what pson soever shall be found vnfaithfull and vniust in the said voyadge by imbecillinge or w<sup>th</sup>drawinge of any the goodes wares m<sup>r</sup>chandizes Iewells or other Cōmodities whatsoeuer either belonging to the adventure sent out or being pcell of the retourne of the same w<sup>ch</sup> was, is, or shall be either p<sup>r</sup>pared bought or belongeing to the Comon or generall stocke or adventure that such pson shall be barred &amp; excluded to demaunde of the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Companie of the said merchaunte of London tradeing into the East Indies any accompte reckoninge or payment of any wage salarye contract or interteinement for his ymployment in the voyadge Wherevnto he was or otherwyse might haue beene interessed yf such offence had not been Comitted. And further that euery such pson soe offendinge shall be psecuted by the said Gouerno<sup>r</sup> and Companie of merchaunte of London tradinge to theast Indies accordinge to the qualitie of their offence in that behalfe by the lawes and statutes of this Realme.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> furthermore we doe ordeine that yf vppon deliu<sup>r</sup>y of her ma<sup>ties</sup> lres to the Princes of those places where o<sup>r</sup> shipps shall arrive you shall be peaceablie received and enterteyned as marchaunte to Comerce and traffique w<sup>th</sup> the people of those Countries or places, and be secured and warranted heareafter to frequent and visite those places. Then we doe ordeine and decree that you shall select out of the youngest sorte of o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> and others intertayned by vs or voluntarilie suffered to goe in the voyadge such and soe many of the aptest and towardest of them as you shall thinke meete and as shall haue best approved them selues fit for such an ymployment to recide and abide in those places where you shalbe soe peaceablie received yf yo<sup>u</sup> may be pmitted therevnto, takeinge sufficient and carefull order for the defrayinge and supplyinge of their chardges untill those places shall be hereafter <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-99" data-page="99" data-folio="7" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n98_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 7">[p. 7]</button> visited w<sup>th</sup> another ffleete sent from hence, and leaueing w<sup>th</sup> them such advise and direc̃cõn for their better informacon how to Carrie themselues in those places as by yo<sup>r</sup> good discrecõn w<sup>th</sup> the advise of such as you shall conferr thereof shall be thought meete &amp; as time and experience of those places shall direct you.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AND forasmuch as the daies of mans lyfe are lymitted and the Certentie thereof for their Continuance and end onelie knowne vnto god Wee the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie of marchauntę of London tradinge to theast Indies doe heereby ordeine and prouide that yf yt shall happen you the said Iames Lancaster to deꝑte this mortall lyfe before the retourne of the said shipps then from and after the decease of you the said Iames Lancaster we doe by this o<sup>r</sup> present Comission vnder o<sup>r</sup> Comon seale constitute and appointe yo<sup>u</sup> m<sup>r</sup> Iohn Middleton<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-005" href="#sec-04-fn-005" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-005" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> to be the cheefe Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> or generall of the said m<sup>r</sup>chauntę marino<sup>rs</sup> Officers and other her ma<sup>ties</sup> subjectę by vs ymployed or otherwyse shipped in the said voyadge willing and requiring you and geving you like power and aucthoritie to putt in execucõn the said ordenancę and decrees conc<sup>r</sup>ninge the orderinge and disposeinge of the traffique and m<sup>r</sup>chandizeinge of the said voyadge as we haue donne to the said Iames Lancaster and yf yt shall happen by Godes appointment that both you the said Iames Lancaster and Iohn Middleton shall decease in the said voyadge then we ordeine and appointe the Principall and gen<sup>r</sup>all gouernm<sup>te</sup> of the wholle Companie their in ymployed or shipped in this present voyadge unto you m<sup>r</sup> Wit̃tm Brund<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-006" href="#sec-04-fn-006" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-006" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> being alsoe one of o<sup>r</sup> ffower principall merchauntę whome we haue chosen and interteined for the orderinge and disposeing of the marchandizes and traffique of the said voyadge requieringe you yf you shall fortune to surviue the said Iames Lancaster and Iohn Middleton to observe and keepe the same o<sup>r</sup> ordenancę and decrees before mencioned ordered and appointed by vs for the traffique and marchandizeing of the said viadge geueing you the like authoritie to execute the same as is formerlie <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-100" data-page="100" data-folio="8" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n99_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 8">[p. 8]</button> hearein geven to the said Iames Lancast[er] &amp; Iohn Middleton or eyther of them &amp; yf you the said Wiłłm Brund shall fortune to decease in the said voyadge then we doe appointe the imediate succession and execucõn of the said chardge and gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>te</sup> afore said vnto you m<sup>r</sup> Iohn Havarde one other of o<sup>r</sup> said Principall m<sup>r</sup>chauntę requiringe you to see o<sup>r</sup> ordenanꝛe and decrees to be kept and ꝑformed geving vnto you the same power and aucthoritie as we haue hereby geven to the said Iames Lancaster Iohn Middleton &amp; Wiłłiam Brund or any of them.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AND yf yt should fortune that all the said seuerall ꝑsons ymployed by vs as o<sup>r</sup> Principall m<sup>r</sup>chauntę in this present voyadge to decease Then we ordeine the wholle gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> and chardge before mencioned to be vndertaken by one of you foure w<sup>ch</sup> are of the second sorte of marchauntę or factor<sup>s</sup> by vs ymployed in the said voyadge the same to be taken and executed successiuelie one after another as any of you shall happen to decease after the said chardge by the true meaninge hereof shall be cast vpon you w<sup>ch</sup> chardge we doe ordeine shall succeede in this manner viz<sup>t</sup> ffirst to o<sup>r</sup> second merchant shipped in the Redd Dragon next to o<sup>r</sup> second merchaunte or ffactor<sup>r</sup> shipped in the Hector, thirdlie to o<sup>r</sup> second m<sup>r</sup>chante or factor<sup>r</sup> shipped in the Assencõn, and lastlie to o<sup>r</sup> second m<sup>r</sup>chante or ffactor<sup>r</sup> shipped in the Suzan.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AND we doe further ordeine that as we haue appointed a succession o<sup>r</sup> principall merchaunte and Gouernor of the wholle Companie imployed or shipped in this voiadge soe we doe order and decree that vppon whomsoeu<sup>r</sup> the said gou<sup>r</sup>nment in succession shall fall by the decease of any the ꝑsons before named that he shall or may shipp or imbarke himselfe in the Admirall of o<sup>r</sup> ffleete &amp; inioye and receave such place Cabbin easment and Comaundm<sup>t</sup> therein as o<sup>r</sup> said generall and principall Gouernor formerlie didd.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AND lastlie wheareas her ma<sup>ty</sup> by her Comission vnder the greate seale hath onelie appointed the generall Gouernment of all her subiectę ymployed in y<sup>e</sup> said voiadge vnto the said Iames Lancaster w<sup>th</sup>out any appointeinge of succescon by like warrant to any that is ymployed in the said voiadge and that yt lyeth not in vs to giue any warrant for the correccon of offences by penall <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-101" data-page="101" data-folio="9" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n100_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 9">[p. 9]</button> Lawes to be executed vpon the bodies of any her ma<sup>ties</sup> subiecte we doe in that behalfe as to men haueing reason and discrec̃on and to men that feare god offer vnto yo<sup>r</sup> good considerac̃ons the beneficte of order and peaceable agreem<sup>te</sup> in matters and enterprises vndertaken for a Comon good, Reposeing in you o<sup>r</sup> seuerall marchaunte and all yo<sup>u</sup> o<sup>r</sup> seuerall Officers appointed and interteyned in this voiadge a spetiall hope trust and Confidence that you will accorde &amp; agree together and ioyne in freindshipp &amp; amytie to doe &amp; execute yo<sup>r</sup> vttermoste endevo<sup>rs</sup> for the benefitt of the voyadge w<sup>th</sup>out contenc̃on, discorde or emulac̃on to be vsed amongst you guideinge yo<sup>r</sup> selues therein by that gen<sup>r</sup>all regiment and Sea gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>te</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> o<sup>r</sup> English ffleete doe use when they sorte them selues together haueing a spetiall and due respect to him that is yo<sup>r</sup> Principall or Cape m<sup>r</sup>chaunte. And soe we Comend yo<sup>u</sup> and yo<sup>r</sup> travels to gode pvidence who guide you w<sup>th</sup> his feare and defend you from all daungers.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">From the Councell to their loueinge freindes Alderman Banninge:<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-008" href="#sec-04-fn-008" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-008" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Alder:</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Holliday:<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-009" href="#sec-04-fn-009" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-009" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Ald: Smyth<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-010" href="#sec-04-fn-010" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-010" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> &amp; the rest of the Adventurers in the voiadge intended to theast Indies for going forward w<sup>th</sup> the voyadg w<sup>th</sup>out feare of any lett.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-007" href="#sec-04-fn-007" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-007" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> o<sup>r</sup> heartie Comendac̃ons: wheareas vppon o<sup>r</sup> tres heertofore written vnto yo<sup>u</sup> touchinge her ma<sup>ties</sup> likeinge of yo<sup>r</sup> intenc̃on to sett forth a voyadge to the East Indies, &amp; alsoe of her gratious allowance and assent to the proiect of Articles and Condic̃ons required for the pformance of the same w<sup>th</sup> an assurance pmysed you that the said voyadge shall receue no stay or interupc̃on, there hath beene sett downe as we are <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-102" data-page="102" data-folio="10" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n101_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 10">[p. 10]</button> geven to vnderstand soe many pticular somes of aduenture vnder the handes of divers Contributors as are thought a Convenient pporcõn or stocke to be sett forth for yo<sup>r</sup> first voiadge &amp; there vppon a pporcõn hath beene made of shippinge victualls &amp; other necessaries, wherein greate somes of money haue been disburced. Notw<sup>th</sup>standing all w<sup>ch</sup>, as we are informed there is some stay made amonge yo<sup>r</sup> selues of the pceedinge in this Action vpon some reporte spred amongest yo<sup>r</sup> selues, that the voyadge shall not be pmitted to goe forward, wherein we wish you to be better advised &amp; not to be soe easilie drawne to make a doubt of her Ma<sup>ties.</sup> assent and assurance soe late by vs Certified vnto you, w<sup>ch</sup> cannot be done without dishono<sup>r</sup> to her ma<sup>tie</sup> or iniurie to vs, from whom you rec<sup>d</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> warrant to proceede: Lett yt therefore suffice that there is noe iuste cause whie you should make any doubt of going forward in yo<sup>r</sup> intended voyadge: But rather that you should therein vse all expediçõn and possible speede to advance the same, knowing that otherwyse you may much preiudice yo<sup>r</sup> selues by yo<sup>r</sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-103" data-page="103" data-folio="11" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n102_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 11">[p. 11]</button> staggeringe and delaies, and yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde any knowne ympediment or practise vsed by any, that shall either w<sup>th</sup>drawe him selfe, or practise to w<sup>th</sup>drawe others from Contributinge that w<sup>ch</sup> hath been once by him or them sett downe and agreed vppon to adventure: wee wish you to advertice vs thereof that we may receiue some good reason by aunsweare from them, that may iustlie mooue them to haue lesse desier now, then they formerlie had to pceed in the said voiadge, for that we hold it to be a publicke Acc̃on not to be dallied withall att mens private pleasures: And thus we bidd you farewell.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A warrant from the lo: Threr for passadge of victuals from porte to porte for provision of the India ships.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-011" href="#sec-04-fn-011" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-011" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> MY hartie Comendãc̃ons wheareas by the sufferaunce &amp; assent of her ma<sup>tie</sup> for the ho: of this Realme and the good of the Comon wealth a voyadge is p<sup>r</sup>paring by c<sup>r</sup>teine m<sup>r</sup>chauntę of London towardes the East Indies, And for the victualling of the ships ymployed in the said voiadge w<sup>th</sup> Biskett, meale &amp; wheate &amp; other victuall the said m<sup>r</sup>chauntę haue appointed one Captaine Baker &amp; Robert Pope to make pvisions as they best cann w<sup>th</sup>in the seu<sup>r</sup>all Counties of Somerset Dorset &amp; Devon &amp; to shipp their provisions frome porte to porte to be brought together to the Portę of Darthmouth or Plymouth to be taken abourde their shipps as the shall come to the said Portę or either of them Theis are therefore to requier you to pmitt and suffer the said Capt: Baker and Rob̄t Pope &amp; either of them, or the berer hereof in their behalfe from tyme to time vntill the said shipps be gon on their voyadge, to lade into any shipp or vessell lieing in any the Portę <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-104" data-page="104" data-folio="12" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n103_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 12">[p. 12]</button> w<sup>th</sup>in the said seu<sup>r</sup>all Counties, any Biskett meale or wheate provided for the said voiadge &amp; to passe the same from porte to porte vppon Bondes taken of them to retourne dew Certificate of the arrivall of the same at such porte in the said seu<sup>r</sup>all Counties for w<sup>ch</sup> the same shall be entred, by them or in their names. And in soe doing this shall be vnto you sufficient dischargde        ffrom Sackvile howse the 12<sup>th</sup> of Nouember 1600.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> loueing ffreind<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-012" href="#sec-04-fn-012" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-012" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">To my verie loueing ffreinde her ma<sup>ties</sup> Officers of the Portes &amp; Creeke w<sup>th</sup>in the seu<sup>r</sup>all Counties of Som<sup>r</sup>set Dorsett &amp; devon and eu<sup>r</sup>y of them to whome this doth or may Concerne.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Councells second order for better paym<sup>te</sup> of adventures &amp; supplies.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">VPPON<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-013" href="#sec-04-fn-013" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-013" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Complainte made this day to their l<sup>le</sup> by the Comitties of the East India Companie that the debte of the present voyadge now by them sett forth amounteing to seaven thowsand poundes aboue the contribucons sett downe by the adventuro<sup>rs</sup> are not paied the same growinge by reason of the backwardnes and froward disposicõns of divers psons that shew them selues remisse and vnwilling to furnish their promised contribucons and alsoe their porcõns of their supplies w<sup>ch</sup> since att their generall meetinge att seu<sup>r</sup>all tymes haue been agreed on to yeald aboue their adventures a rate of iiij<sup>s</sup> in the pound for the cleireing of the said debt. Their Lo<sup>ps</sup> vppon due Consid<sup>r</sup>acõn how much yt importeth the hono<sup>r</sup> of the state that soe publique an accõn should not receave scandall and shamefull imputacõn Haue this day ordered that forasmuche as the said debt in all or in parte is proportionablie due to be satisfied by eu<sup>r</sup>y Contributo<sup>r</sup> of the voyadge. That <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-105" data-page="105" data-folio="13" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n104_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 13">[p. 13]</button> therefore the said Companie and every adventuro<sup>r</sup> thereof shall w<sup>th</sup>out further deley not onelie pforme and satisfie their full contribucõns agreed vpon promysed and set downe under their handę But alsoe their proportionable supplies according to their agrementę at their generall Courtę or otherwyse yf any pson or psons shall refuse to conforme them selues therevnto that then the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> deputie and Comitties of the said Companie or any fower of them shall by vertue of this their Lo<sup>ps</sup> order streightlie inioyne and Comaund the said psons to make their imediate apparance before their Lo: to thend their Lo<sup>ps</sup> may pceede to the printinge of their contempt by refusinge to accomplish their owne agrem<sup>t</sup> &amp; promyse soe much imparteing the publicke as their obstinacie &amp; pversnes shall deserve. <em>And to that end</em> direccõn is geven to a messenger of her ma<sup>ties</sup> Chamber to attend the said Gouerno<sup>r</sup> and Companie to bringe the said pties before their łł: .</p></div><figure class="archival-plate" id="fig-008"><img src="/figures/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/fig-008_p0105.webp" alt="Decorative drop capital E with foliate and fruit pattern" loading="lazy"></figure><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Warrant for the Coyning<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-014" href="#sec-04-fn-014" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-014" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> of money for the East India voyadge.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">LIZABETH by the grace of God &amp;c To o<sup>r</sup> Trustie and welbeloued Thomas Knevet Esquier warden of o<sup>r</sup> Mynte, Richard Martin Knight, &amp; Richard Martyn his sonne m<sup>rs</sup> and workers of o<sup>r</sup> Moneys w<sup>th</sup>in o<sup>r</sup> Tower of London and ells wheare within o<sup>r</sup> Realme of England, &amp; to Richard Rogerbe Comptroller Thomas Denham provost of o<sup>r</sup> Mynte w<sup>th</sup>in o<sup>r</sup> said Tower of London &amp; to all other the Officers &amp; Mynisters of o<sup>r</sup> said Mynte w<sup>th</sup>in o<sup>r</sup> said Tower of London to whome it shall appteine &amp; to every of them greetinge. ffor asmuch as <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-106" data-page="106" data-folio="14" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n105_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 14">[p. 14]</button> we by the advyse of o<sup>r</sup> Councell for c<sup>r</sup>taine causes and Consideracõns vs moueinge Haue fullie resolved and determined to cause to be now newlie made stricken and Coyned w<sup>th</sup>in o<sup>r</sup> said Mynte c<sup>r</sup>teine money &amp; Coyne out of Bullion plate and forreine sylver, to be made stricken and Coyned w<sup>th</sup> y<sup>e</sup> p̄cullies on thone syde, &amp; o<sup>r</sup> Armes of England on thother syde at c<sup>r</sup>taine values and ratę by vs appointed therefore &amp; heerafter in theis presentę expressed or mencioned w<sup>ch</sup> money and coyne is principallie by vs intended to be for thuse traffique and trade of c<sup>r</sup>teine o<sup>r</sup> marchauntę latelie by vs incorporated by the name of Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Companie of m<sup>r</sup>chauntę of London trading into the East Indies &amp; the same money to be made of the ffines of Eleaven ouncę twoe penny weight of fine sylver &amp; eighteene penny weight of allay in the pound w<sup>t</sup> of Troy as is specified in certaine Indentures beareing date the 30<sup>th</sup> day of Ianuarie in the 25<sup>th</sup> yeare of o<sup>r</sup> reigne, conc<sup>r</sup>ning makeing and coyning of certeine other o<sup>r</sup> moneys of gold and siluer p̄ticulerlie mencioned in the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-107" data-page="107" data-folio="15" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n106_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 15">[p. 15]</button> same Indentures, w<sup>ch</sup> Indentures are made betweene vs on the one p̱tie, &amp; the said Richard Martyn: now Knight by the name of Richard Martyn Cittizen and Alderman of London, then Warden &amp; m<sup>r</sup> &amp; worker of o<sup>r</sup> said moneys on the other p̱te, as in the same Indentures plainelie is expressed and contayned. And that alsoe the said money soe newlie to be made stricken &amp; coyned by force of theis presente, keepe in nomber a hundred and Nyne Testerns in the pound weight Troy aforesaid to be Coyned into Testerns of eight, Testerns of fflower, Testerns of twoe and single Testerns. And because o<sup>r</sup> said printe &amp; moneys aforesaid by vs and o<sup>r</sup> Councell now as aforesaid intended to be newlie made stricken &amp; Coyned for the intente and purposes aforesaid, cannott continuallie be made according to the right standerd in as much as some tyme in default of the said m<sup>rs</sup> or workers their deputie or deputies yt may be made to stronge or too feble by too much or too little in fines or in both We pondering &amp; Consideringe the same, will therefore &amp; straightlie Comaund you and every of you, o<sup>r</sup> said Officers of o<sup>r</sup> Mynte to whome yt shall appteine. That when the said moneys vppon the assay before the deliuerie thereof be founde too stronge or too ffeble, all onelie in weight, or all onelie in fines or in both by two penny weight, in the weight of one pound Troy aforesaid, after the ould Computac̃on of Twentie penny weight in the ounce &amp; noe more, the w<sup>ch</sup> two penny weight in that one, or two penny weight in that other, or two penny weight in both, shall be called remedie for the said m<sup>rs</sup>. That then such moneys be deliuered for good, soe always as the same default happen by casualtie and otherwyse not. But yf default be found in the said moneys in weight or in fines or in both over the said two penny w<sup>t</sup> in the pounde weight aforesaid. That then the deliu<sup>r</sup>aunce of the said moneys soe Coyned &amp; stricken cease &amp; that money to be challenged lesse then the good, &amp; at the Coste of the said m<sup>rs</sup> to be new molten &amp; reforged, till yt be putt to printe as money deliu<sup>r</sup>able.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And we</span> will That o<sup>r</sup> said Warden in respect of the said moneys soe newlie to be made stricken and Coyned as aforesaid shall receiue &amp; take for vs, aswell of o<sup>r</sup> owne Bullion as of the Bullion of any other p̱son or p̱sons whatsoeuer of everie pound weight of Troy soe made and Coyned Two and Twentie pence <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-108" data-page="108" data-folio="16" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n107_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 16">[p. 16]</button> starlinge by nomber out of w<sup>ch</sup> xxij<sup>d</sup> the said m<sup>rs</sup> shall receiue for their workmanship &amp; for all other manner of waste, provisions &amp; other necessaries in &amp; aboute the making of the said moneys xiiij<sup>d</sup> by Tale, &amp; soe shall remaine vnto vs vpon euery pounde weight soe Coyned viij<sup>d</sup>, out of w<sup>ch</sup> the said warden, Controller &amp; other Officers of o<sup>r</sup> said Mynte shall receiue their ffees by the hand of o<sup>r</sup> said Warden, w<sup>ch</sup> o<sup>r</sup> will &amp; pleasure is shall be paied in manner &amp; forme as in the said mencioned Indentures beareing date the said xxx<sup>th</sup> day of Ianuarie in the said xxv<sup>th</sup> yeare of o<sup>r</sup> Reigne &amp; a scedule to the said Indenture annexed is lymitted &amp; appointed, &amp; that for the same o<sup>r</sup> warden shall be chardged and accomptable to vs &amp; o<sup>r</sup> heires &amp; Successo<sup>rs</sup> in such manner as he is &amp; ought to be, for that w<sup>ch</sup> is or ought to be cominge to vs by force of the said Indenture bearing date the said xxx<sup>th</sup> daye of Ianuarie in the said xxv<sup>th</sup> year of o<sup>r</sup> Reigne of or for the Coynadge of the sylver moneys made &amp; Coyned by vertue of the said Indentures. And o<sup>r</sup> will &amp; pleasure is &amp; we doe straightlie commaund &amp; chardge you &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y of you o<sup>r</sup> said Officers of o<sup>r</sup> Mynte to whome yt shall apptaine That from tyme to tyme you cause to be well and trulie entred &amp; recorded in one Lieger &amp; melting booke all such Bullion of Silver as from tyme to tyme shall be brought into the saied Minte to be Coyned by force of theis p<sup>r</sup>sente, w<sup>ch</sup> entrie shall comprehend the weight &amp; ffines of the same Bullion, the pties name that brought yt in, &amp; what day, &amp; to be assayed tried &amp; pixed &amp; the rateing &amp; the melting thereof don ou<sup>r</sup>seene &amp; vouched as by the said former Indenture bearing date the said xxx<sup>th</sup> of Ianuarie in the xxv<sup>th</sup> yeare of o<sup>r</sup> Reigne is limitted &amp; appointed to be don for the moneys of sylver therein mencioned. And that aswell we o<sup>r</sup> selues, as alsoe the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Companie of m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London trading into the East Indies shall haue &amp; inioye all &amp; all manner such &amp; as ample libties benefitte &amp; Advantages to all intente &amp; purposes for and concerning the said fower new moneys of sylver soe newlie to be made strocken &amp; Coyned by force of theis presente and eu<sup>r</sup>y of them respectiuelie, rateablelie &amp; pportionablie as we or they should might or ought to haue by the intent &amp; true meaninge of the said former Indentures for the moneys of sylver therein mencioned. And that the said wardens, M<sup>rs</sup>, &amp; all and euery of <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-109" data-page="109" data-folio="17" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n108_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 17">[p. 17]</button> o<sup>r</sup> said Officers of o<sup>r</sup> said Mynte shall in all thinge doe &amp; pforme &amp; cause to be don and pformed for &amp; concerning the said fflower moneys soe newlie to be made stricken &amp; Coyned by force of theis presente as well towarde vs as towarde euery of o<sup>r</sup> said m<sup>r</sup>chaunte, all &amp; all manner matters &amp; thinge whatsoeuer, w<sup>ch</sup> ought to be any way don or pformed by them or any of them, by their or any of their meanes by vertue of the said former Indenture for or concerning the moneys of Sylver therein mencioned &amp; according to the true intent &amp; meaneing of the same Indentures.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AND inasmuch as o<sup>r</sup> pleasure is, that diligent expedĩcõn be had &amp; don in that behalfe: We Will &amp; Comaund you &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y of you to whome yt shall appertaine that from henceforth w<sup>th</sup> all diligence from tyme to tyme vntill such tyme as we declare vnto you the said Warden, m<sup>rs</sup>, woorkers Comptrollers &amp; other Officers &amp; Ministers of o<sup>r</sup> Mynte o<sup>r</sup> pleasure in writeing to the contrarie you doe make stricke Coyne and printe, or cause to be made, stricken Coyned &amp; printed, into the said moneys soe newlie to be made stricken &amp; Coyned by force of theis presente. All such Bullion plate sylver in masse or otherwyse as shall be deliuered vnto yo<sup>r</sup> hande according to the true intent and meaning of theis presente by a pticular Warrant to be signed by vs, or directed or subscribed w<sup>th</sup> the names of three of o<sup>r</sup> privie Councello<sup>rs</sup> for the tyme being att the least whereof the Lord Threr̄ for the tyme being to be one.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A warrante.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AND THE same moneys soe made stricken &amp; Coyned to deliuer or cause to be deliuered to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie of m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London trading into the East Indies that shall bringe in the same their Attornes &amp; deputies in such Convenient tyme &amp; in such manner and forme as by the said Indentures for the moneys therein expressed is lymitted &amp; appointed. And theis o<sup>r</sup> łres Pattente w<sup>th</sup> the said warrant soe signed or directed as aforesaid for yo<sup>r</sup> workeinge of o<sup>r</sup> moneys in manner &amp; forme aboue specified shall be aswell to you o<sup>r</sup> said warden m<sup>rs</sup> and workers of our said moneys, as alsoe to all &amp; singuler other o<sup>r</sup> Officers in o<sup>r</sup> said Mynte yo<sup>r</sup> deputie or deputies sufficient warrant &amp; cleare dischargde in this behalfe, any Couenante, graunt, article, clause, or agreem<sup>t</sup> had made or exspressed in the said Indentures beareinge date the xxx<sup>th</sup> day of Ianuarie in the xxv<sup>th</sup> yeare of o<sup>r</sup> reigne <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-110" data-page="110" data-folio="18" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n109_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 18">[p. 18]</button> betweene vs on thone pte, &amp; the said Richard Martyn knight on thother parte, or any other Acte, Statute, Ordinance or pmyse had or made to the Contrarie heareof in any manner of wyse notw<sup>th</sup>standinge. In Witnes wheareof &amp;c: <span class="sc">wheareas</span><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-015" href="#sec-04-fn-015" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-015" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> you the severall psons herevnder named haue beene divers tymes required to bringe in yo<sup>r</sup> promysed Contribucons towardes the settinge forth of the East India voyadge, and haue not yett donne the same, we doe eftsoones requier you to giue yo<sup>r</sup> direct aunsweare whether you will bringe into one of the Thrērs of the Companie yo<sup>r</sup> said Contribucons or not (yf not) then accordinge to your warrant of the lłē of her Ma<sup>ties</sup> privie Councell to vs in that behalfe geaven<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-016" href="#sec-04-fn-016" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-016" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> we require you to repaire to the Courte and yeald to their Lo<sup>ps</sup> a reason of yo<sup>r</sup> refusall, and yf you will assent to paie yo<sup>r</sup> Contribucons then to repaire to me the Gouerno<sup>r</sup> of y<sup>e</sup> Companie and to giue yo<sup>r</sup> worde for the present payment of the same   dated the 22<sup>th</sup> of April 1601.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><ul class="editorial-list"><li>Richard Champion<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-017" href="#sec-04-fn-017" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-017" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup>                         Robert Pennington Hugh Hamersley<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-018" href="#sec-04-fn-018" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-018" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup>                           Richard Hearne <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-111" data-page="111" data-folio="19" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n110_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 19">[p. 19]</button> Willm Kellett	Willm Palmer Willm Barrett	Robert Myldmay Barth: Huggett<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-019" href="#sec-04-fn-019" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-019" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup>	John Bate Peter Helinge	Robert Bowyer</li></ul></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A letter to the kinges of the East Indies.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Elizabeth</span><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-020" href="#sec-04-fn-020" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-020" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> by the grace of God Queene of England ffraunce &amp; Ireland defendo<sup>r</sup> of the faith &amp;c: To the greate and mightie kinge of Whearas almightie God in his infinite and vnsearchable wisedome and gratious providence hath soe disposed of his blessinge and of the good thinge of this world created and ordayned for thuse of man, that the same how ever they be brought forth &amp; doe either originallie growe and are gathered, or otherwyse Composed &amp; made some in [one] Countrie and some in another: yet are they by the Industrie of man directed by the hand of God dispersed and sent out into all the partes of the world, that his wonderfull bountie in his Creatures may appeare vnto all Nãcõns, his Maiestie haueing soe ordaned that noe one place should inioye (as the Natiue Comodities thereof) all thinge apptayninge to mans vse, but that one Countrie should haue need of another, &amp; out of the aboundance of the ffruite w<sup>ch</sup> some region enioyeth the necessities or wante of another should be supplied: By w<sup>ch</sup> meanes men of seu<sup>r</sup>all &amp; <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-112" data-page="112" data-folio="20" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n111_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 20">[p. 20]</button> ffar remote Countries haue comerce and traffique one w<sup>th</sup> another, &amp; by their interchandge of Comodities are lincked together in amitie &amp; freindshipp.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">THIS CONSIDERAtion most noble kinge, together w<sup>th</sup> the ho: reporte of yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> for the well entertayninge of Straungers that visitt yo<sup>r</sup> Country in loue &amp; peace w<sup>th</sup> lawfull traffique of Marchandize, haue moued vs to giue licence to diuers o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte who haue beene stirred vpp w<sup>th</sup> a desier by a longe and daungerous Navigacõn to finde out &amp; visitt yo<sup>r</sup> Territories &amp; dominions being famous in theis pte of the world for honorable m<sup>r</sup>chandizeing, &amp; to Offer you Comerce and traffique in buying bartering and enterchandgeing of Comodities w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> people according to the Course of m<sup>r</sup>chaunte w<sup>ch</sup> Commerce and enterchandging, yf yo<sup>r</sup> [ma<sup>tie</sup>] shall accept of &amp; shall receiue &amp; entertayne o<sup>r</sup> marchaunte w<sup>th</sup> favo<sup>r</sup> according to the hope that gaue them encouragm<sup>t</sup> to attempt soe long and daungerous a voiadge you shall finde them a people in their dealing &amp; Conversacõn of that Iustice &amp; Ciuillitie that you shall not mislike of their repaire to yo<sup>r</sup> dominions, &amp; vpon furder Conference &amp; inquisicõn had w<sup>th</sup> them both of the kinde of their m<sup>r</sup>chandize brought in their shipps and of other necessarie Com̄odities w<sup>ch</sup> o<sup>r</sup> dominions may afford: yt may appeare to yo<sup>r</sup> Ma<sup>tie</sup> that by their meanes you may be furnished in their next retourne into yo<sup>r</sup> porte in better sorte then you haue beene heretofore supplied, either by the Spaniard or Portugall, who of all other Nacõns in the parte of Europe haue onelie hitherto frequented yo<sup>r</sup> Countrie w<sup>th</sup> trade of m<sup>r</sup>chandize &amp; haue been the onelie ympedim<sup>te</sup> both to o<sup>r</sup> subiecte and diuers other Marchaunte in the parte of Europe that they haue not hitherto visited yo<sup>r</sup> Countrie w<sup>th</sup> trade, whilest the said Portugalls p<sup>r</sup>tended them selues to be the souereigne Lorde &amp; Prince of all yo<sup>r</sup> Territories, &amp; gaue yt out that the held yo<sup>r</sup> Nacõn &amp; people as Subiecte to them: &amp; in their stiles and Titles doe write them selues kinge of the East Indies.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AND yf yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> shall in yo<sup>r</sup> princelie ffavo<sup>r</sup> accept w<sup>th</sup> good likeing this repaire of o<sup>r</sup> m<sup>r</sup>chaunte vnto yo<sup>r</sup> Countries, resorting thither in peaceable traffique &amp; shall intertaine this their first voyadge as an introduccõn to a furder Continuance of League and freindshipp betweene yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> &amp; vs &amp; of Comerce &amp; entercourse betweene yo<sup>r</sup> subiecte &amp; ours We haue geven Order to this <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-113" data-page="113" data-folio="21" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n112_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 21">[p. 21]</button> o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchaunt (yf yo<sup>r</sup> Ma<sup>tie</sup> should be pleased therew<sup>th</sup>) to leaue in yo<sup>r</sup> Countrie some such of o<sup>r</sup> said Marchaunte as he shall make choice of to recide in yo<sup>r</sup> dominions vnder yo<sup>r</sup> princelie &amp; saffe p̄teccon vntill the retourne of another ffleete, w<sup>ch</sup> we shall send vnto you, who may in the meane tyme learne the languadge of yo<sup>r</sup> Countrie &amp; applie their behavier as yt may best sorte to so Converce w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>ties</sup> subiecte to thend that Amitie and freindshipp being intertayned and begunn, the same may the better be Contynued when o<sup>r</sup> People shalbe instructed how to direct themselues according to the ffashions of yo<sup>r</sup> Countrie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> because in the Consideracon of the entertayning of Amytie &amp; freindshipp in the establishinge of enterco<sup>r</sup>se to be contynued betweene vs there may be required on yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>te</sup> behalfe such promyse or capitulacon to be p̄formed by vs, w<sup>ch</sup> we cannot in theis o<sup>r</sup> łres take knowledge of, We therefore pray yo<sup>r</sup> Ma<sup>tie</sup> to giue eare therein to this bearer and to giue him Creditt in whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> he shall pm̄yse or vndertake in o<sup>r</sup> name concerning o<sup>r</sup> Amitie &amp; entercourse w<sup>ch</sup> promyse we for o<sup>r</sup> ptę in the worde of a Prince will see p̄formed &amp; will be redie gratefullie to requite any loue kindnes, or fauo<sup>r</sup> that o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte shall receiue at yo<sup>r</sup> Ma<sup>te</sup> hande. Praying yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> for o<sup>r</sup> better satisfaccon of yo<sup>r</sup> kinde acceptaunce of this o<sup>r</sup> loue &amp; amitie Offered yo<sup>r</sup> highnes you would by this bearer giue testimonie thereof by yo<sup>r</sup> princelie łres directed vnto vs, w<sup>ch</sup> shall giue vs greate &amp; wonderfull Contente   And thus &amp;c.</p></div><h3 class="sec-h2">ARTICLES OF AGREEMENT<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-021" href="#sec-04-fn-021" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-021" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> indented [and] made betweene the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie of m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London trading to the East Indies on the one p̄te and George Waymouth of in the Countie of      marriner on the other p̄te the      daie of Aprill 1602.</h3><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-114"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="114" data-folio="22" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n113_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 22">[p. 22]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Whereas</span> the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie of the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London trading into the East Indies vpon greate deliberac̃on had &amp; taken of the longe &amp; tedious course w<sup>ch</sup> hath been hitherto houlden by all such as doe trade or sayle from theis pt̃e of the world into the East Indies alonge the Coaste of Europe and Africa by the Cape of Bona Esperansa and of the greate Aduentures w<sup>ch</sup> are borne in soe longe a voyadge by many kindes of daungers offered therein &amp; beinge moued w<sup>th</sup> greate hope that there is a possibilitie of discou<sup>r</sup>y of a neerer passadge into the said East Indies by Seas by the way of the Norwest yf the same were vndertaken by a man of knowledge in Navigac̃on and of a resoluc̃on to put in execuc̃on all possibilitie of industrie &amp; valo<sup>r</sup> of the atteyninge of soe inestimable benefitt to his natiue Countrie &amp; his owne ppetuall hono<sup>r</sup> Haue to that end enterteyned the saide George Waymouth aman in their opinion qualified &amp; ffitt to vndertake and attempt the pformance of this discouerie vnto whome they haue deliu<sup>r</sup>ed beforehand the some of One hundred pounde to furnish himselfe w<sup>th</sup> Conveient instrum<sup>t</sup>e of Navigac̃on accordinge to his owne choyse &amp; vnto whose direcc̃on the haue comitted two shipps the one called the Discou<sup>r</sup>y the other the Godspeede being manned victualled prepared and ffurnished w<sup>th</sup> all thinge necessarie and convenient for such a voyadge and theirin bestowed and supplied all kinde of provisions according to his owne desier, whereby both the said George Waymouth and his Companie are prouided of victualls apparell and furniture for the space of 16 monnethes and haue alsoe Laden abourd the said shippes a Convenient pporc̃on of seu<sup>r</sup>all kinde of m<sup>r</sup>chandize. In Considerac̃on whereof yt is agreed betweene the said pties to theis present in manner &amp; forme following on the other side.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><span class="sc">The Covenantes</span> agreed vpon viz.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Capteine Way: &amp; his Comp: to sayle towardes ffretam Davis &amp; soe forward by the Norwest to the kingdomes of Cataya or China or the backe-side of America.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">not to retourne of one whole yeare att the least.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">The</span> said George Waymouth doth promyse couenante and agree to and with the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Company of the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London tradinge into the East Indies that he and his Companie shall and will be redie by the daye of next ensueing the date hereof to departe from the porte of London and as wynd and weather <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-115" data-page="115" data-folio="23" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n114_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 23">[p. 23]</button> will ꝑmitt shall &amp; will directlie sayle towarde the Coaste of Groineland into that pte of the Seas w<sup>ch</sup> is discribed in sundry generall Mapps by the name of ffretam Davis and shall passe on forwarde in those Seas by the Norwest or as he shall finde the passadge best to lye towarde the parte or kingdomes of Cataya or China or the backe side of America w<sup>th</sup>out geueing ouer the proceedinge on his Course soe longe as he shall finde those Seas or any pte thereof navigable and any possibilitie to make way or passadge thorough them And shall not him selfe retourne or voluntary suffer any of his Companie to retourne backe againe vnto or towarde the Coast of England for any lett or ympedim<sup>te</sup> whatsoeuer vntill he and they haue bestowed one yeare att the least from the tyme of their depture hence in going forward seeking sounding and attemptinge the pforme of this intended viage.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Tolleracon of such as y<sup>e</sup> Companie shall appointe to keepe Registers of the goodes &amp; observacons of their proceedinges in the voyadge.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Iournall of their proceedinges to be deliured to the Com-</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">panie by the Capt: w<sup>th</sup>in 10 daies after his retourne.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Not to discouer his proceedinges in the voyadge otherwyse then to the Gournor &amp; Company.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> further the said George Waymouth doth promyse graunte and agree to and w<sup>th</sup> the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Company that he the said George Waymouth shall and will not onelie well enterteine &amp; intreate: but alsoe quietlie pmitt and suffer such marchaunte or Pursars as the said Gouerno<sup>r</sup> &amp; Companie shall place or appointe to goe in the said shipps or either of them, to keepe a register and accompt of all such goodes wares m<sup>r</sup>chandizes pvision and furniture as are shipped and Laden in any the said shipps how the same is bestowed bartered chandged spent or otherwyse disposed &amp; alsoe to keepe an Accompte of all other thinge that shall be bought bartered exchandged or receiued in Lewe of any thinge that shall be sold or made away &amp; alsoe to make note Iournalls &amp; observac̃ons in writeing of their Continuall pceedinge in the said viadge vntill their retourne for England for the direcc̃on &amp; helpe of posteritie that shall be occasioned or willinge heereafter to make a new attempt vpon the same discouery. And further that he the said George Waymouth shall w<sup>th</sup>in 10 daies after his retourne into England whether he doe pforme the said discou<sup>r</sup>y or not w<sup>th</sup>out concealem<sup>t</sup> of any thinge w<sup>ch</sup> he hath discou<sup>r</sup>ed in the viadge deliuer a declarac̃on in writinge vnder his hand vnto the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-116" data-page="116" data-folio="24" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n115_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 24">[p. 24]</button> Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of the said Companie, or his deputie conteyneinge a reporte of all &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y his pceedinge in the viadge worthie of note or memory for the good of the Companie and for the helpe of such as shall be disposed heereafter to proceed in the same passadge, and shall be redie from tyme to tyme duringe the space of 40 daies after his arriuall &amp; retourne to London vpon warninge and somons geven him in that behalfe to come before the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; deputie of the said Company for the tyme being &amp; the Comitties &amp; such others of the Company as yt shall please the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; dep: to call vnto them &amp; shall trulie relate vnto them such thinge as passed in the said viadge whereof they or any of them shall desier to be enformed w<sup>th</sup> out denyall or refusall in that behalfe &amp; shall not discouer the secreete or course of his pceedinge in the vyadge to any other pson or psons whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> then to the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> deputie &amp; Comitties.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">500li graunted to the said Cap: after proofe he hath discoured the said passadge.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie of the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte of London trading to the East Indies for them and their Successo<sup>rs</sup> doe in consideracõn of the premisses promyse couenant and agree to &amp; w<sup>th</sup> the said Georg Waymouth to satisfie &amp; pay vnto the said George Waymouth or his Assignes w<sup>th</sup>in ffortie daies after his retourne into England and after sufficient proofe and testimoniall by him made that he hath passed thorough the North west passadge into the East Indies and arriued at any porte w<sup>th</sup>in the dominions of the kingdomes of Cataya, China<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-022" href="#sec-04-fn-022" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-022" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup>, or Japan the some of ffiue hundred pounde of lawfull English money w<sup>th</sup>out fraude or Coven.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-023" href="#sec-04-fn-023" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-023" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The said Capt doth disable him selfe from all demaundes for his sallary &amp; paines takinge yf he discour not.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> lastlie the said George Waymouth doth promyse and agree that vnles in this intended viadge he shall discou<sup>r</sup> &amp; passe thorough the same Northwest passadge &amp; shall make sufficient proofe and giue good testimoniall that he hath passed through the same passadge and arriued in some parte of the East Indies in <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-117" data-page="117" data-folio="25" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n116_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 25">[p. 25]</button> the dominions or kingdomes of Cataya China or one of them that then neyther he the said George Waymouth nor his Assignes shall or will demaund or requier of the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> or Company or any of them any Salary wage or rewarde for his viadge or travell in the discou<sup>r</sup>y of the said passadge in reguarde the said viadge was vndertaken by the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Company ptelie by his pswa<span class="sc">c</span>õn and vpon his resolu<span class="sc">c</span>õn to adventure his travell &amp; lyfe therein for the good of his Countrie to w<sup>ch</sup> his resolu<span class="sc">c</span>õn the said Companie were content to add the Aduenture of the setting forth of the viadge to their greate chardge. In Witnes &amp;c:</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A lre from m<sup>r</sup> Doctor Cæsar to the Comp:</aside></div><p class="prose-p">FTER my hartiest Comenda<span class="sc">c</span>õns: where[as] this inclosed peti<span class="sc">c</span>õn<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-024" href="#sec-04-fn-024" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-024" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> hath beene exhibited to the kinge ma<sup>tie</sup>, his highnes finding that yt was a cause meerlie appteyninge to the Courte of Admiraltie referred the same vnto me to take order therein accordinglie, and forasmuch as there is an attachm<sup>t</sup> made out of the said Courte of Admiraltie vpon c<sup>r</sup>teyne goode in yo<sup>r</sup> hande att the suite of the peticioners who exhibited the said peti<span class="sc">c</span>õn. Theis are therefore to praie &amp; requier you to make staie of the said attached goode in yo<sup>r</sup> hande vntill such time as the cause shall be examined by me accordinge to his ma<sup>ties</sup> Comaundm<sup>t</sup> or that you shall receiue further order from me touchinge the same Whereof requireing you not to faile I hartilie bid you ffarewell from the Courte of Wilton this 27<sup>th</sup> of October 1603.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">yo<sup>r</sup> verie loueing ffreind</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><em>Julius Cæsar.</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-025" href="#sec-04-fn-025" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-025" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-118"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="118" data-folio="26" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n117_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 26">[p. 26]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Answeare to Doctor Cæsars lre.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Right</span> wo<sup>ll</sup> It maie please yo<sup>u</sup> to be aduerticed that we receiued a łre from yo<sup>r</sup> wo<sup>p</sup>: wherein was inclosed a petic̃on exhibited to his ma<sup>tie</sup> by the m<sup>r</sup> and Company of the Suzan one of the shipps latelie retourned from the East Indies, wherein the peticioners haue vntrulie sugested to his ma<sup>tie</sup> that the East India ffleete weare Ioinctlie and in one coniunction &amp; łre of Marte vnder the greate seale of England aswell for Civill gouernm<sup>t</sup> as Reprisall bound for the East Indies, whereas indeede the same ffleete weare whollie and altogether set [out] for the discou<sup>r</sup>y and trade of Marchandize and to that end weare furnished w<sup>th</sup> diuers Natiue commodities of the Realme and other provisions fitt to procure and settle a trade in those pte and all the Marriners w<sup>ch</sup> were ymployed in the said voyadge weare hired and intertayned for wage by the Monneth as is vsuall in all m<sup>r</sup>chauntę voyage. All w<sup>ch</sup> shipps were at the dispotic̃on &amp; direcc̃on of their Generall then M<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster and sithence graced by his ma<sup>tie</sup> of a knight, who according to his Comission as generall Agent &amp; directo<sup>r</sup> of this voyadge and intended trade did vpon his arrivall in the East Indies dispose and appointe the m<sup>r</sup> &amp; Companie of the Suzan to goe in trade of m<sup>r</sup>chandize to a place called Pryamon<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-026" href="#sec-04-fn-026" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-026" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> in the said Indies &amp; frome thence to retourne directlie for England The Gen<sup>r</sup>all himselfe purposinge w<sup>th</sup> the other two shipps to visitt other partes of the said Indies and haueing notice of a Portugall Gallion that laye in the waye to those places which he intended to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-119" data-page="119" data-folio="27" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n118_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 27">[p. 27]</button> visitt as all the Marrino<sup>rs</sup> of his owne shipp, &amp; of the other two shipps in Companie w<sup>th</sup> him were hired by the monneth to pforme a Marchaunte voyadge and weare not to attempt any enterprize of reprisall vnlesse they were spetiallie compounded w<sup>th</sup>all did then make a contract w<sup>th</sup> all the Marriners of the Redd Dragon, the Hecto<sup>r</sup> and the Assenc̃on what shares the should haue in such prize as should be taken by them then in Consortshipp together, and afterwardes pceedinge in their voyadge did meete w<sup>th</sup> the said Gallion and did surprize her accordinglie.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-027" href="#sec-04-fn-027" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-027" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> By reson of w<sup>ch</sup> Contract the said Generall since his arrivall in England did satisfie and paie all the Marriners of the said 3 shipps present in that service such shares and allowances as by Composic̃on was agreed vpon betweene them together w<sup>th</sup> the monnethlie wage accordinge to their Continuance in the voyadge, w<sup>ch</sup> monnethlie wage the said m<sup>r</sup> &amp; Marrino<sup>rs</sup> of the Suzan according to the tyme of their being in the voyadge haue likewyse receiued, and being not present at the said service or enterprize for w<sup>ch</sup> a speciall Composic̃on &amp; Consortshipp was made weare not to ptake in the said Composic̃on. And this we hope shall suffice to satisfie yo<sup>r</sup> wo<sup>p</sup>: y<sup>t</sup> theis men complaine w<sup>th</sup>out cause, and clayme an interest in that whereto y<sup>e</sup><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-028" href="#sec-04-fn-028" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-028" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> haue noe colo<sup>r</sup> of right haueing alredie rec<sup>d</sup> their wage according to agreem<sup>t</sup> for the wholl tyme of their ymploym<sup>t</sup> in the said voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The L: Thrers lre to S<sup>r</sup> Thom: Smyth Govrnor &amp; the Comp: the 5 of Nouem: 1603.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER MY very hartie Comendac̃ons Wheareas I am aduerticed by the Officers of the porte of London that there was entred in the name of you and the Company to the quantitie of 1030000 pound weight of pepp w<sup>ch</sup> was latelie taken out of those shipps come from the East Indies<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-029" href="#sec-04-fn-029" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-029" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-120" data-page="120" data-folio="28" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n119_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 28">[p. 28]</button> wherein you weare Adventurers and haue (as is affirmed) howsed and laid vpp accordinglie, fforasmuch as noe pte or pcell of the said pepp is to be sold before his ma<sup>ties</sup> greate Masse of pepp now remayneing att Leaden Hall be first vttered and sold according to his ma<sup>ties</sup> good plesure in that behalfe signified &amp; expressed in my late lres sent vnto the said Officers. I am therefore to will and requier you vppon yo<sup>r</sup> dutie and Allegeaunce to his ma<sup>tie</sup> to Certifie me forthw<sup>th</sup> y<sup>e</sup> seu<sup>r</sup>all names of them who weare in any sorte owners or interessed pties in the said pepp soe taken out &amp; entered as aforesaid, and alsoe where the same doth now remayne to thend the sale thereof may be stayed and prevented till his highnes pepp be first vented &amp; disposed of as appteyneth, ffor you are to knowe that his ma<sup>tie</sup> maie by vertue of his princelie p<sup>r</sup>ogatiue as his highnes hath beene advised by the learned Iudge of this Land &amp; his owne counsell in the lawe iustlie staie the vent &amp; vtterance of all other pepp vntill his owne be first sold &amp; vttered &amp; for yo<sup>r</sup> better satisfacōn in this pointe yf you will reteyne and bringe yo<sup>r</sup> Councell to Winchester at the begininge of this terme you &amp; the both shall be heard, and his ma<sup>ties</sup> Councell indifferentlie by the Iudge, &amp; then I doubt not you will be well satisfied of his Ma<sup>ties</sup> lawfull power and prerogatiue in this behalfe Soe not doubtinge of yo<sup>r</sup> dew reguarde to be had herevnto I bidd yo<sup>u</sup> hartelie farewell. frome my howse att Horselie this 5 of Nouember 1603.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> loueing ffreind</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">T: Buckhurst.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-030" href="#sec-04-fn-030" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-030" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Companies aunsweare to y<sup>e</sup> L. Thrers lre y<sup>e</sup> vijth of Nouember 1603.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">UR HUMBLE duties remembred. It may please yo<sup>r</sup> Lo<sup>p</sup>: to be aduerticed that we haue accordinge to yo<sup>r</sup> lre here inclosed a note of the names of all such of o<sup>r</sup> Comp: as haue receiued into their hande any of the pepp w<sup>ch</sup> is retourned in o<sup>r</sup> shipps from the East Indies and doe humblie herewith informe yo<sup>r</sup> lo<sup>p</sup>: That we vpon the comeing home <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-121" data-page="121" data-folio="29" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n120_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 29">[p. 29]</button> of o<sup>r</sup> shipps being to paie vnto the Marriners retourned in this vyadge for soe much dew vnto them the some of 30000 [    ] and haueing noe meanes by the vente of o<sup>r</sup> Cōmodities retourned to rayse any moneys towardes the paym<sup>te</sup> thereof by reason of the present contagion &amp; mortalitie in the Cittie<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-031" href="#sec-04-fn-031" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-031" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup>   After we had made tryall of makinge<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-032" href="#sec-04-fn-032" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-032" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> vpp of moneys vppon the Creditt of men of best estate in the Cittie and could not obteyne yt at any rate we are driven to agree to devide the pepp amongst o<sup>r</sup> selues thereby to furnish every man w<sup>th</sup> some meanes to bringe in money towardes paym<sup>t</sup> of this greate some dew to the said Marrinos<sup>rs</sup> and towardes the setting out of o<sup>r</sup> shipps againe to fetch home a good pte of goodes lefte in the Indies &amp; such ffactors and Marriners as are left behinde in the Country W<sup>ch</sup> meanes yf it shalbe crossed by any restrainte that the Adventurers cannot vtter their pepp allotted vnto them after three yeares forbeareing of their moneys in this vyage herevpon will ffollowe the Clamo<sup>r</sup> of Marrinos<sup>rs</sup> for not paym<sup>te</sup> of their wage a Cessinge and giueing ouer of a new voyadge to be sett forth, the losse of all the goode w<sup>ch</sup> remayne in the East Indies to be fetcht home, and a losse of the liues of agreate nomber of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors &amp; Marriners left there with the princes of those pte, w<sup>th</sup> whome they be left as pledge for the retourne of o<sup>r</sup> shipps thither againe this yeare: We are right Hono: easilie pswaded w<sup>th</sup>out Contradicc̃on that his ma<sup>tie</sup> by his princelie prerogatiue may staie the vent &amp; vtteraunce of o<sup>r</sup> pepp retourned but yf his ma<sup>tie</sup> shall vse his prerogatiue in this case o<sup>r</sup> trade standing in such extremitie, herevpon will likewyse ensue an vtter desisting &amp; giueing ouer of a trade verie chardgable begonne verie likelie to haue proued beneficiall to o<sup>r</sup> Countrey &amp; kingdome and disgrace fullie geven over w<sup>th</sup> the losse of the liues of soe many of his Ma<sup>ties</sup> subiecte, w<sup>ch</sup> otherwyse might be made men of spetiall vse in this Comon wealth, haueing gaiened soe greate experience in trade &amp; Navigac̃on.   Besides yf the said Adventurers shall not be pmitted to make sale of the said pepp the Companie shall be depriued of meanes to paie his ma<sup>tie</sup> the custome of the said goode w<sup>ch</sup> cometh to 5000 [    ] or their about whereof yt may please yo<sup>r</sup> Lo<sup>p</sup>: to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-122" data-page="122" data-folio="30" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n121_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 30">[p. 30]</button> haue Consideracõn and to afford vs the ffurtheraunce in o<sup>r</sup> busines after this greate discontinuance of traffique in this Cittie w<sup>ch</sup> may enable vs to doe service to his ma<sup>tie</sup> and Comon wealth in o<sup>r</sup> places and callyng<em>e</em>.
