And thus humblie releyinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> Lo<sup>ps</sup> good favo<sup>r</sup> towardes vs Wee take o<sup>r</sup> leaue of yo<sup>r</sup> good Lo<sup>p</sup>: London the vij<sup>th</sup> of Nouember 1603.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A lre from the L Threr to the Company.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">HAUING pvsed yo<sup>r</sup> lres I impted the effect thereof vnto my Lordes And as it is iust and convenient that all resonable reguarde be had towardes you, who w<sup>th</sup> soe greate chardge and adventure doe advaunce his ma<sup>ties</sup> Customes &amp; vente the Comodities of the Realme and speciallie that such paym<sup>te</sup> as are of necessitie to be made by you should w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> owne be satisfied (yf neither yo<sup>r</sup> present store of money nor yo<sup>r</sup> Credit be able otherwyse to dischardge the same w<sup>ch</sup> seemeth somewhat straunge to be soe [ )], evensoe I doubt not but as you are wyse men and faithfull Subiect<em>e</em> to the Crowne and state, soe you will not be soe pciall to yo<sup>r</sup> priuate [good] as altogether to forgett the publicke w<sup>ch</sup> alwaies liueth in the pson of o<sup>r</sup> kinge and Sou<sup>r</sup>eigne, and whose onelie prosperitie is the publicke wealth and benefitt of vs all. I maye not deny that many inconvenience &amp; clamo<sup>rs</sup> are like enough to follow yf yo<sup>r</sup> Marriners should be vnsatisfied and an iniury to the kinge yf that his Customes should rest vnpaied, but that there is noe way to satisfie the one and to paie the other but by this port sale of pepp soe ill for the kinge and (of all other the worst waye for yo<sup>r</sup> selues [ )], that see I not any reason to beleeue but on the other side you alsoe must confesse that yf yo<sup>r</sup> pepp haue his sale before the king<em>e</em> be vnterred<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-033" href="#sec-04-fn-033" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-033" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> it will soe greatlie impeach his ma<sup>ties</sup> markett, as either his must abide a longe time vnsold, or being presentlie offered will ryse to his extreame losse. And what hugh paym<sup>te</sup> the kinge hath now to make (whereof a good portion might by agood sale ofhis highnes pepp be dischardged) &amp; what Clamo<sup>rs</sup> &amp; Complaint<em>e</em> are daylie are made by <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-123" data-page="123" data-folio="31" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n122_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 31">[p. 31]</button> those to whome moneys are dewe &amp; w<sup>ch</sup> by reason of this generall Contagion (his ma<sup>ties</sup> Rentę Customes, debtę ffifteenes &amp; subsidies being not paied) cannot possibly by his ma<sup>tie</sup> be dischargded of all w<sup>ch</sup> Clamo<sup>rs</sup> and inconveniencę thus falling likewyse vpon theis affaires ofhis ma<sup>tie</sup> you must yf you will be indifferent<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-034" href="#sec-04-fn-034" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-034" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> haue sence and feelinge of theis distasters of the kinge as of yo<sup>r</sup> owne Wherefore to conclude the matter beinge of such importaunce both towardę his ma<sup>tie</sup> and yo<sup>r</sup> selues and the kingę highnes in his most gratious &amp; respectiue goodnes towardes his subiectę being resolute to doe noe mann wronge, &amp; most desireous to releiue his owne estate in a dew co<sup>r</sup>se of right &amp; Iustice, and yet w<sup>th</sup> as little losse as may be effected for you, I am vpon theis groundę wished by my Lordę first to requier you to staie sale of yo<sup>r</sup> pepp vntill such order therein may be taken as may be both w<sup>th</sup>out preiudice to his ma<sup>tie</sup>, and to yo<sup>r</sup> good satisfaccõn besides. And next that since the effectinge hereof requiereth a speedie resolucõn, that therefore you doe forthw<sup>th</sup> send hither to Winchester nowe while the lodę: in respect of theis arraignem<sup>t</sup>ę doe tarry heare a couple of discreete ꝑsons, or moe yf you soe thinke good who may be both authorised and instructed by you to debate deale and conclude for and on yo<sup>r</sup> behalfe, w<sup>th</sup> such of the Lordes as by his ma<sup>tie</sup> shall be likewyse aucthorised therevnto. And soe eftsoones prayinge you to doe y<sup>t</sup> w<sup>th</sup> all speede possible for that the llę come hither the 14<sup>th</sup> daie &amp; heere tarrie the 15 · 16 · 17 · 18 &amp; on the Satterdaie being the 19 retourne to the Courte &amp; on the 21 · 22 · remayne att Winchester againe. Soe as w<sup>th</sup>in the Compasse of this tyme theis ꝑsons insteucted &amp; authorized must come. And soe I end w<sup>th</sup> all hartie well wishinge vnto you <em>Winchester</em>.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-035" href="#sec-04-fn-035" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-035" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-124"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="124" data-folio="32" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n123_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 32">[p. 32]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Comp: aunswere to the L. Thrers lre.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">RIGHT Hon: and my verie good Lo: vpon the receipte of yo<sup>r</sup> hono<sup>rs</sup> lre of the xiij<sup>th</sup> present, I caused a meeteing of soe many of the m<sup>r</sup>chauntę Comitties &amp; traders into the East Indies as weare p<sup>r</sup>sent to be found w<sup>th</sup>in the Cittie &amp; ympted vnto them yo<sup>r</sup> hono<sup>rs</sup> pleasure w<sup>th</sup> the sight of yo<sup>r</sup> lre but not being able at the present to finde soe many as might giue warrauntę for the sending of such men w<sup>th</sup> Comission to Winchester as yt pleaseth yo<sup>r</sup> Lo: to Comaund to giue their attendaunce vpon you and the rest of the Lordę did determyne w<sup>th</sup> all speede to send into the Country being nigh vnto the Cittie for such Comitties as are absent to make their repaier. and soe we are most humbly to craue yo<sup>r</sup> hono<sup>r</sup> ffavo<sup>r</sup> to pdon vs till we should be able to meete together and then p<sup>r</sup>sent order shall be taken for that such men may be sent as yt pleaseth yo<sup>r</sup> Lo<sup>p</sup>: to wryte for w<sup>ch</sup> shall be w<sup>th</sup> in the appoynted tyme lymitted in yo<sup>r</sup> Hono<sup>rs</sup> lre all the Aduenturers relying vpon yo<sup>r</sup> Lo<sup>ps</sup> good favo<sup>r</sup> towardę them  And soe I humblie take my leaue of yo<sup>r</sup> good Lo<sup>p</sup>: London the 15<sup>th</sup> of Nouember 1603.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">yo<sup>r</sup> Lo<sup>ps</sup> most humble att Comaund <em>Th:</em></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><em>Bramley.</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-036" href="#sec-04-fn-036" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-036" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-125"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="125" data-folio="33" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n124_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 33">[p. 33]</button></div><h3 class="sec-h2">S<sup>r</sup> IAMES Lancasters remembraunce left in the East Indies.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-037" href="#sec-04-fn-037" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-037" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></h3><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Remembrance for M<sup>r</sup> Keche m<sup>r</sup> of the pinnace.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">firste</span> as soone as I am gone you shall p̱cure from the m<sup>r</sup>chauntę resident heare yo<sup>r</sup> ladeing of such and soe much goodę as they shall appointe you and by all meanes after you haue taken them in p̱cure such pvisions that they may be kept drie, for a little wett bringeth in theis goodę much damadge. I doubt not you will be carefull in this pointe.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">YO<sup>r</sup> ladeing being rec<sup>d</sup> you are to take in m<sup>r</sup> Towerson<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-038" href="#sec-04-fn-038" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-038" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Thomas Tudd<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-039" href="#sec-04-fn-039" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-039" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> Will<sup>m</sup> Chase<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-040" href="#sec-04-fn-040" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-040" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> &amp; Thiūs Dobson<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-041" href="#sec-04-fn-041" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-041" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> and then sale directlie for the Iland of Banda not touching in any place by the waie w<sup>th</sup>out greate cause &amp; wheresoeu<sup>r</sup> you become trust none of the Indians for their bodies &amp; soules be whollie treason and yt will be very daungerous to touch in any vnknowne place therefore avoied yt.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">WHEN god shall send you to Banda &amp; [you] haue deliu<sup>r</sup>ed yo<sup>r</sup> m<sup>r</sup>chandizes yo<sup>u</sup> maie laie vpp yo<sup>r</sup> shipp and you and yo<sup>r</sup> men <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-126" data-page="126" data-folio="34" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n125_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 34">[p. 34]</button> lie att the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte howse &amp; assist them in their busines all that you cann, but I thinke yt good to looke to yo<sup>r</sup> shipp that she be not lost, but husband her soe that yf occasion should be proffered by any enymies a land you maie haue some helpe by her by the Sea.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">As</span> my trust is in you, assist the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte, and giue them all helpe for I haue placed you in the 4<sup>th</sup> place w<sup>th</sup> them. In this pointe be verie carefull as I hope you will be &amp; hereby I doe acknowledge to haue Consorted w<sup>th</sup> you from the j of febr 1602. for as longe as you shall be in the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte service for 8<sup>li</sup> the monneth. Thus God send his blessinge upon yo<sup>u</sup> in all yo<sup>r</sup> affaires. in <em>Bantam</em> this 12 of ffebr 1602.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Remembraunce for m<sup>r</sup> Starkie Tho: Morgan &amp; m<sup>r</sup> Scott.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-042" href="#sec-04-fn-042" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-042" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">As</span> sone as o<sup>r</sup> shipps be dispatched from hence w<sup>th</sup> as much Convenient speede as may be made, I would haue you to dispatch the Pinnace for the Moloccos to the Iland of Banda &amp; lade in her of all sorte of Comodities as you can learne to be vented there, onelie keepe yo<sup>r</sup> narrowe &amp; mallacoe ffarde: to furnish you here as yo<sup>r</sup> most staple Comodities to trust vnto, you may lade in her some 60 ffardells, the most of blewes &amp; checkered stuffe, some fine Pinthadoes, &amp; some store of Corse, some of yo<sup>r</sup> Sian rowles: but not many, &amp; some white Cloth &amp; some browne &amp; some of yo<sup>r</sup> stuffe, that be Checkered, in all theis thinge vse yo<sup>r</sup> good Iudgm<sup>te</sup> onelie lett them be well furnished.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ALSOE lade in her 500<sup>R</sup> of 8 att the least for yt may chaunce they maie haue neede to vse some money.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">THE next business that most importeth is yo<sup>r</sup> dispatch of yo<sup>r</sup> howse yf yo<sup>u</sup> could sett forward the one in dispatching the other <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-127" data-page="127" data-folio="35" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n126_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 35">[p. 35]</button> yt would doe very well, onelie haue this reguard in the building theis yt be as free from fire as chardge will make yt.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">FFOR the sales of yo<sup>r</sup> Comodities, you must vse yo<sup>r</sup> owne discrecõns alwaies holdinge this in mynde the tyme of the comeing of o<sup>r</sup> shipps and [procure] 20,000 bagge of pepp as neere as you cann to be in readynes agayst their comeing and asmuch money as will dispatch them here of chardge for this next accompte, I will not haue willinglie to be mingled w<sup>th</sup> any other, If yt please god to send me home I will wryte to you att lardge aboute this pointe for yt will ymport vs that are ymployed in this busines, myne opinion is to putt yo<sup>r</sup> selues out of Comodities as soone as you cann &amp; putt yo<sup>r</sup> selues into pepps for in soe doinge you may chaunce to benefitt the Company in the defraying yo<sup>r</sup> chardge for they shall be at aboue 850<sup>li</sup> the yeare chardge in theis parte.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">WHEN you haue bought any store of pepp yf the Holland shipps come and the prise ryse you may take the benefitt &amp; sell to the Companies most proffitt onelie haue a reguard not to be vnfurnished at the expectacõn of o<sup>r</sup> shippinge. You haue the benefitt of 2 harveste, I doubt not but you shall furnish the next shipps in good sorte.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ALSOE I doe appointe you m<sup>r</sup> Willm Starkey to be head &amp; cheefe Commaunder ou<sup>r</sup> all in this place but to take the Councell and advise of Thomas Morgan &amp; m<sup>r</sup> Scott in all matters that the Companie may be the better assured of all thinge that passeth.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AND yf yt please god [to lay his hand] vpon you m<sup>r</sup> Starkey &amp; to take you out of this world I would haue you to giue ouer yo<sup>r</sup> chardge &amp; accompte to Tho: Morgan. And yf you Tho: Morgan die then to m<sup>r</sup> Scott, &amp; yf you m<sup>r</sup> Scott then to whom you shall thinke moste meete, alwaies leaueing yo<sup>r</sup> thinge in the best manner you cann &amp; the playnest.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">FOR such wage as shall be due to men y<sup>t</sup> be here you may allowe yt by the yeare, or halfe yeare as you see mens necessities allwaies haue reguard that you be not necessited for want of money lett not to haue 1000 R of 8 at the least att all tymes by you.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AND for the boyes lett them not waunt apparrell &amp; necessaries that appteyne vnto them for soe they shall doe yo<sup>u</sup> better service Lett the Cooke boye be bound for 7 yeares &amp; to haue the ffreedome of London in his tyme accompte the 2 yeares y<sup>t</sup> he hath served in o<sup>r</sup> shippe.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-128"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="128" data-folio="36" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n127_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 36">[p. 36]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Thus</span> I end desiering you to meete together in the Morninge &amp; eueninge in prayer, god whom yee serve shall the better blesse you in all yo<sup>r</sup> affaires. Thus I wysh his blessing to be alwaies vpon you to p<sup>r</sup>serue and keepe you &amp; to putt his feare in yo<sup>r</sup> harte. <em>Amen.</em></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Forget</span> not to husband yo<sup>r</sup> Comodities that there be nothinge spoyled nor come to decaye vnder yo<sup>r</sup> hande.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Remembraunce for Tho: Tudd, Gabriell Towerson, W<sup>m</sup> Chasse, and Thom: Dobson.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Assoone</span> as the Pynnace is laden by the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte resident here in <em>Bantam</em> of such and soe many ffardells of Cloothes and Pynthados as they shall appointe yo<sup>u</sup> shall imbarke yo<sup>r</sup> selues in her &amp; by the helpe of m<sup>r</sup> Ketch &amp; his Company saile directlie w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> said goode for Banda vnlesse you touch in some place necessarie for yo<sup>r</sup> refreshing and make no longe stay and haue espetiall care of yo<sup>r</sup> saffetie and how you putt any man a land in any place for the people in those partes their wholle bodyes and minde be all treason &amp; therefore open yo<sup>r</sup> eyes in this behalfe.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> when god shall send you to Banda take a howse or howses for yo<sup>r</sup> busines as you shall thinke most ffitt for the Companies best proffitt and make sale of yo<sup>r</sup> Comodities alwaies advaunceing the price the best you may.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">In</span> yo<sup>r</sup> provision you shall make in Nutmegge and maces haue you agreate care to receiue such as be good, for the smallest &amp; Rotten nuttmegge be worth nothinge at home, soe that their ffraight and principall will be lost. of Maces the fayrest and best will be soonest sould and to best Rekoninge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Alsoe</span> be carefull to gett together all the Cloues you can and vse all diligence to pcure some 60 or 80 Tonns att the least &amp; the rest of Nutmegge &amp; maces. All theis thinge must be carefullie husbanded w<sup>ch</sup> you must haue a spetiall reguard vnto.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">I Make</span> accompte the Companie will fraight some 2 shipps for that place of the burthen of 600 Tonns more or lesse &amp; therefore haue a care to get their ladinge in tyme and aforehand, that yt may be redie by such tyme as the shipps be w<sup>th</sup> you w<sup>ch</sup> I hope will be Michaellmas come tweluemonneth or before, Yt doth greatlie ymporte you to be carefull and pcure ladinge, for this is yo<sup>r</sup> whole busines there &amp; therefore ar yo<sup>u</sup> sent.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-129"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="129" data-folio="37" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n128_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 37">[p. 37]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ALSOE I would haue you to agree together loueinglie like sober men, for yo<sup>r</sup> owne discordes yf you suppress them not will be to the m<sup>r</sup>chauntę greate losse and hindrance and to yo<sup>r</sup> owne vndoing. Therefore Gou<sup>r</sup>ne yo<sup>r</sup> selues soe that there be noe brabbles amonge you for any cause.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">I HAUE appointed m<sup>r</sup> Ketch to be an assistant to you in you<sup>r</sup> busines and vse him as one of yo<sup>r</sup> selues ymploying him in yo<sup>r</sup> busines as you shall haue most neede.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">IF you send him to any place lett yt not be farr of &amp; w<sup>th</sup>out daunger of enymies for I would not haue him farr from you whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> should befall, neither will I haue y<sup>t</sup> he putt himselfe in any daunger.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ALSOE I doe appointe Thomas Tudd to be cheef ffactor and principall and the rest to be at his Comaund in this busines, next him I appointe Gabriell Towerson, and next Gabr: Towerson <em>Wm Chasse</em>, &amp; nex Willtm <em>Chasse</em>, <em>Tho: Dobson</em>, and you <em>Tho: Tudd</em> I would haue you to take councell of the rest in all this busines that eu<sup>r</sup>y mans advise may be geven to the benefitt of the voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ALSOE my will is that yf yt please god to call you Thomas Tudd out of this world then Comitt the busines, and yo<sup>r</sup> place to m<sup>r</sup> Towerson and yf you Gabr: Towerson die Comitt yo<sup>r</sup> place to W<sup>m</sup> Chasse, &amp; yf yt fortune you Willtm Chasse to die Comitt the ffactory to m<sup>r</sup> Ketch m<sup>r</sup> of the pinnace, and yf he doe chaunce to die lett him Comitt yt to Thomas Dobson.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ALSOE I haue geven order to the ffactor<sup>rs</sup> heare to supplie you w<sup>th</sup> 500 royalls of 8 because we knowe not what neede you may haue, &amp; haue a care to yo<sup>r</sup> money &amp; dispose yt not till greate occasion serue.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AND for the paying of yo<sup>r</sup> Companie their wage, doe yt sparinglie &amp; remitt soe much as you cann till their retourne, but when necessitie requireth rather doe yt in Comoditie then money yf you in yo<sup>r</sup> discrecõns see such occasion.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">I WOULD haue you to paie noe wage to <em>Thomas</em> the barber, and fitt him w<sup>th</sup> Clothes meete not to exceede in any thinge for he is an other mans prentise &amp; seeketh onelie to wast his masters.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-130"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="130" data-folio="38" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n129_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 38">[p. 38]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ALSOE I would haue you Tho: Tudd to keepe the Accompte of all such busines as shall passe.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">p me<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-043" href="#sec-04-fn-043" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-043" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Tho: Tudd p Ga: Towerson p W<sup>m</sup> Chasse.</p></div><h2 class="sec-h1">LRES into the East Indies from the Company in December 1603.</h2><h3 class="sec-h2">Laus dei in London the 2 of decemb̃ 1603.</h3><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To oʳ loueing freind Mr Wm Starkie English mʳchaunt Resident att Bantam.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER oʳ verie hartie Comendãcõns: yoʳ seuʳall łres of the 10ᵗʰ of Nouember &amp; the 16ᵗʰ of ffebruarie 1602 the one dated at Achem, the other att Bantam, we haue recᵈ wᵗʰ the Accompte therein menciōed, wheareby we doe pceaue the state of oʳ busines left vnder yoʳ chardge, whearein we pray God to blesse &amp; prosp yoᵘ, to thend wᵗʰ all good success &amp; saffe retourne that we may therewᵗʰ Congratulate yoʳ comeing into yoʳ owne Country and more pticulerlie be informed of the state of the trade wᵗʰ mutuall Conference wᵗʰ yoᵘ, then yt is possible we should be by the intercourse of oʳ łres. At yoʳ depture out of England you may remember the state of oʳ Countrie was troubled in the Cause of the deceassed Earle of Essex since whose death and yoʳ depture hence God hath taken away oʳ late Queene, &amp; although therein he visited this kingdome wᵗʰ a Capitall triall, yet he left vs not thereby in any garboile<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-044" href="#sec-04-fn-044" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-044" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> or trouble, but instantlie vpon the death of the Queene, wᵗʰ sollempne pclamãcõn first in diuers places in London &amp; afterwarde in the seuʳall Counties of the Realme was <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-131" data-page="131" data-folio="39" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n130_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 39">[p. 39]</button> p̃clamed kinge of England Iames the first before kinge of Scotte w<sup>th</sup> soe generall a Consent that noe oppotic̃on or question was once p̃ticularlie made to the Contrarie, &amp; theirby this kingdome soe peaceablie established that this wonderfull blessinge of god upon vs, was Admired by all the neighbor<sup>s</sup> kingdomes adioyneinge: Notw<sup>th</sup>standinge since as tymes haue their chandge and as god doth many tymes humble his people lest they should forgett them selues in prosperitie, yt hath pleased him to chastice this kingdomes w<sup>th</sup> greate sicknes &amp; mortalitie in diuers place thereof espetiallie in the Cittie of London where there hath died betweene the 7 of December 1602 &amp; the first of december 1603 . 38138 . people, w<sup>th</sup>in the Cittie of London &amp; the liberties &amp; subvrbes of the same, &amp; as may appeare by the Bills of the weeklie reporte of people deceased their died in one weeke in the Cittie liberties &amp; subvrbes 3385 p̃sons by occasion of w<sup>ch</sup> mortalitie trade hath vtterlie ceased w<sup>th</sup>in the Cittie for almost this halfe yeare, &amp; m<sup>r</sup>chaunte &amp; all others of any estate and Accompt haue depted into the Countrie: yet now god be thanked, doe resorte vnto the Cittie againe the Contagion being well seased, soe as the nomber that now die of al diseases in the Cittie &amp; subvrbes are aboute 200 a weeke The w<sup>ch</sup> sicknes and Contagion, as yt hath pleased God in some good measure to abate soe we beseech him for his m<sup>r</sup>cies sake vtterlie to take away &amp; remoue in his good tyme. And touching o<sup>r</sup> shipps they are all God be thanked retourned in saffetie, &amp; their goode in good Condic̃on, howsoeu<sup>r</sup> the tyme &amp; greate quantitie of goode of the same kinde brought from the Indies by the Hollenders yealdeth but slowe sale and vtteraunce of them. But we hope for better oportunities of sale when men may freelie and saffelie resorte to the Cittie. The meane while our trade being to be Continued &amp; followed, we are preparing w<sup>th</sup> all speede o<sup>r</sup> fower shipps to retourne againe to the Indies and to set forward in their voyadge, aboute 2 monnethes hence by gode assistaunce at the furdest, therefore doe vse the oportunitie of this Conveyaunce<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-045" href="#sec-04-fn-045" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-045" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> to pray you to p̃forme all care and expedic̃on that such lading as is or shall be prepared by the order of S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster our late generall left w<sup>th</sup> you att Bantam for the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-132" data-page="132" data-folio="40" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n131_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 40">[p. 40]</button> reladeing of o<sup>r</sup> shipps homeward may be made redie that the shipps be not occasioned to staie for their ladeinge, and that you pvse dilligentlie his instrucc̃on sent herew<sup>th</sup>, and that you will be carefull that the pepꝑ Cloues &amp; Nuttmegge be well clensed of dust before the be laden abourd for the dustines of them beside the pesteringe and chardginge of the shipp w<sup>th</sup> vnproffitable dust, the Comoditie is disgraced by the vncleannes of yt, and we are desirous that reguard be had as farr as possible may be, that the pepꝑ be lardge and smooth &amp; that the Nutmegge be clensed from Rumps, &amp; that noe Comoditie be brought from soe longe avoyadge, that shall not be cleane &amp; Comend yt selfe. And thus much together w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> good care wherevpon we relie shall suffice for advise touching the p<sup>r</sup>paringe of the goode to be retourned. And as conc<sup>r</sup>ning yo<sup>r</sup> pticular moc̃on wherein you referr yo<sup>r</sup> selfe to o<sup>r</sup> Conference w<sup>th</sup> m<sup>r</sup> Will<sup>m</sup>s &amp; Raph Allen, we purpose to take a tyme before the comeing away of o<sup>r</sup> shipps to Confer w<sup>th</sup> them therein before the comeinge away of o<sup>r</sup> shipps, who haue beene all this tyme of sicknes in the Countrie, &amp; are not yett retourned to London. And thus for this time we forbeare any further to enlardge, onelie wishinge you both for yo<sup>r</sup> owne pticuler to haue a reguard to the climate wherein you liue &amp; soe to haue a care of yo<sup>r</sup> health, &amp; to remember &amp; admonish others that are w<sup>th</sup> you, to doe the like, &amp; soe comend o<sup>r</sup> selues &amp; all you and yo<sup>r</sup> busynes to the prouidence of God whoe guide you and yo<sup>r</sup> acc̃ons in his feare Amen.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To or loueinge freind m<sup>r</sup> Tho: Morgan English marchaunt Resident att Bantam the 2 of December 1603.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">After</span> o<sup>r</sup> hartie Comendac̃ons. Yo<sup>rs</sup> of the 17<sup>th</sup> of ffebruarie 1602 We haue rec<sup>d</sup> o<sup>r</sup> shipps being all retourned thanke be to God saffe &amp; their goode in good Condic̃on. The tyme heare by reason of agreat mortalitie &amp; Contagion of the plaggue in diuers places of the Realme, &amp; espetiallie in the Cittie of London hath beene verie vnsesonable for trade all marchaunte &amp; others of any accompt haueing depted the Cittie all the sommer in w<sup>ch</sup> tyme there died betweene <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-133" data-page="133" data-folio="41" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n132_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 41">[p. 41]</button> the 17<sup>th</sup> of December 1602 &amp; the 10<sup>th</sup> of December 1603<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-046" href="#sec-04-fn-046" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-046" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> to the number of 38138 psoñs w<sup>th</sup>in the Cittie liberties and subvrbs thereof, But god be thanked the Contagion is very well seased, &amp; there dieth not now of all diseases aboue 200 a weeke whereas there died in one weeke this sommer to the number of 3385 psoñs &amp; neare the nomber of 3000 dyvers weekes togither. Beside this visitac̃on [God] tooke away vpon the 24<sup>th</sup> of March last o<sup>r</sup> late Queene yet did he not leaue vs w<sup>th</sup>out a souereigne for even the same daie was proclamed o<sup>r</sup> kinge Iames the first before kinge of Scotte whose proclamac̃on had noe one mouth that gaine said him, an entraunce and succession admired by all the kingdomes aboute vs, god blesse him &amp; giue him a prosperous reigne ou<sup>r</sup> vs. The state of o<sup>r</sup> trade being by Godes prouidence thus prosperouslie begunn, &amp; we purposeing by Godē grace to retourne o<sup>r</sup> 4 shipps back agayne for the fetching home of the rest of o<sup>r</sup> goodē &amp; to Continue furder trade into the ptē of the East Indies according to the ffactory begunn by o<sup>r</sup> Generall S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster we haue thought yt meete to giue you to vnderstand that o<sup>r</sup> shipps shall godwillinge be redie to departe hence att the furdest w<sup>th</sup>in theis 2 monnethes, &amp; therefore we pray [you] according to the direcc̃on and remembraunce left w<sup>th</sup> you by the said S<sup>r</sup> Iames &amp; now renued by him in his lres sent by this Conveyaunce, to prepare &amp; make redie the goodē appointed for their retourne, that the shipps be not occasioned to stay longe vpon their ladeinge. And for such goodē as are prouided as pepp̃ of the smoothest &amp; lardgest sorte Cloues &amp; Nutmeggē we pray you to haue acare to clense them &amp; free them as much as ys possible from dust &amp; the nutmeggē from Rumps that the Comodities w<sup>ch</sup> shall be retourned be cleane, w<sup>ch</sup> doth much Comend the sale of them, &amp; besidē yt doth free o<sup>r</sup> shipps of an vnproffitable burthen w<sup>ch</sup> would fill vpp the bulke that might bestowe in place there of Comoditie of value. You may conceiue our desier w<sup>ch</sup> is y<sup>t</sup> o<sup>r</sup> goodē be laid abourde cleane &amp; in good Condic̃on wherein we referr o<sup>r</sup> selues to yo<sup>r</sup> care thereof. And for yo<sup>r</sup> moc̃on touching yo<sup>r</sup> entertaynem<sup>te</sup> and Continuance in o<sup>r</sup> ymploym<sup>t</sup> we would willinglie haue aunsweare[d] yo<sup>r</sup> desier by theis o<sup>r</sup> lres, but m<sup>r</sup> Kenton being by reason of the sicknes not <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-134" data-page="134" data-folio="42" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n133_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 42">[p. 42]</button> yett retourned or setled in the Cittie we could not haue tyme to Conferr w<sup>th</sup> him therein, but must referr you vntill the comeing of o<sup>r</sup> shipps by w<sup>ch</sup> we will advise what hath passed in o<sup>r</sup> Conference in that matter And being desierous to giue you all good Contentm<sup>t</sup>, doe purpose to send you such provysion of Oile &amp; wyne for yo<sup>r</sup> health &amp; Comforte as you haue writt for. Praying you to be carefull of yo<sup>r</sup> selfe and to haue a reguard to the Quesines of that Clymate &amp; aire. And soe wishing you all good successe in yo<sup>r</sup> busynes we Comitt you to God.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A lre from the Lo: Threr the last of Nouember 1603.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">After</span><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-047" href="#sec-04-fn-047" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-047" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> o<sup>r</sup> hartie Comendaqons whereas there hath beene alredie proponed by me the L: Thr̄er in the behalfe of the kinge ma<sup>tie</sup> to you the Gou<sup>no</sup>r &amp; Company of Aduenturers trading [into] the East Indies theis three thinge Ensuynge, first that although his ma<sup>tie</sup> by vertue of his princelie p<sup>r</sup>rogatiue maye lawfullie restraine the sale of yo<sup>r</sup> pepp latelie brought frome the East Indies vntill his owne Bulke &amp; Masse of pepp now remaineing att Leaden Hall be first sold and vented, as by his ma<sup>ties</sup> learned Councell his highnes hath beene fullie resolved yet neu<sup>r</sup>thelesse such is his ma<sup>ties</sup> gratious favo<sup>r</sup> &amp; inclinaqon towardes you, haueing respect to yo<sup>r</sup> soe worthie Adventure made &amp; greate Chardges susteyned in this last longe voyadge by you sett forth, soe much for the Hono<sup>r</sup> of his ma<sup>tie</sup> &amp; the publique good of the Realme as he is pleased to forbeare the vsinge of his p<sup>r</sup>rogatiue for this tyme, &amp; to omitt his owne proffitt to giue meanes of benefitt vnto you. Secondlie that there should be a ioynt sale of his ma<sup>ties</sup> pepp and a like quantitie of yo<sup>rs</sup>, &amp; soe to be vttered &amp; sold together equallie &amp; not otherwyse. Thirdlie yf you should thereof mislike then you to buy his ma<sup>ties</sup> pepp at some reasonable rate to be agreed vpon. Now for as much as we vnderstand that like as you doe humblie and thankfullie acknowledge his ma<sup>ties</sup> greate favo<sup>r</sup> towarde you, in not pressing his said prerogatiue <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-135" data-page="135" data-folio="43" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n134_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 43">[p. 43]</button> vpon you. Soe you haueing considered of the two other offers, you doe w<sup>th</sup> all humblenes and thankefullnes embrace the second, namelie to conioyne in sale w<sup>th</sup> his highnes being not able as you pretend to proceede w<sup>th</sup> the latter, w<sup>ch</sup> is to buy the whole, Theis are therefore to signifie vnto you that his Ma<sup>tie</sup> being informed of theis thingę, is well pleased to allow of yo<sup>r</sup> said choise namelie of a Ioynct sale aswell of his ma<sup>ties</sup> pepp as of yo<sup>rs</sup> in a p̄portionable quatitie together. And for the better accomplishing of the same aswell for the kingę benefitt as yo<sup>r</sup> owne behoofe, wee haue thought good to recomend the whole ordering and manageinge of this busines to yo<sup>r</sup> good diligences, Cares, &amp; discrec̃ons praying and requiring you to assemble yo<sup>r</sup> selues together, &amp; to Consider of some such Course for the Carriadge of the same as may produce good effect of some spedie &amp; reasonable benefitt vnto both, ffor the ffurtheraunce of w<sup>ch</sup> wee thinke y<sup>t</sup> requisite first that there be an Inhibition gen<sup>r</sup>all that noe pepp should be brought into this kingdome, by either English or Straunger vntill the said pepp proportionablie agreed vpon as is aforesaid to be sold be first vented, Secondlie y<sup>t</sup> all such pepp as is alredie brought in either by Straungers or English out of the lowe Countries or from the straightę should be sequestred lykewyse from sale. Thirdlie that a present Survey be made of all such pepp as you the Adventurers haue alreadie deliu<sup>r</sup>ed or shall deliu<sup>r</sup> vpon the diuident to p̄ticular Adventures of yo<sup>r</sup> Company w<sup>th</sup> spetiall chardge that noe p̄te thereof be sold vntill his ma<sup>ties</sup> p̄te And yo<sup>rs</sup> p̄portionablie be first vttered. Prouided alwayes that yt may be lawfull for any to transport pepp out of this Realme at their pleasure. And yt seemeth alsoe most iust &amp; reasonable that strict order be taken w<sup>th</sup> the Grocers that they buye noe pepp but his ma<sup>ties</sup> and yo<sup>rs</sup> the same being whollsome &amp; saleable pepp &amp; to that end that a present surveie be made of such store &amp; quantitie as the said grocers now haue vpon their handę. Theis pointę we haue thought ffitt to remember vnto you &amp; that we maye w<sup>th</sup>all speede be adu<sup>r</sup>tised from you aswell of yo<sup>r</sup> opinion in theis thingę as of such other matters whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> as you shall thinke requisite to be further added and deuised for the better accomplishinge of this service, whereof soe soone as we shall haue significac̃on we shall p<sup>r</sup>sentlie giue order for the speedie expedic̃on thereof as <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-136" data-page="136" data-folio="44" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n135_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 44">[p. 44]</button> appteyneth  Soe we bid you hartelie farewell from the Courte att Wilton the the last of Nouemb̃. 1603.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yoʳ verie loueing freinde</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Suffolke           Buckhurst      E Worcester Cumb̃land        Devonshire     W: Knollys HHoward          Ro: Cecill     E Wotton Stanhope         I ffortescue   I Popham</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The Companies aunsweare to th: llˢ lre.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">RIGHT hon: oʳ duties most humblie remembred: we haue accordinge to yoʳ Loˢ: Commission to vs directed mett and Conferred together of some course to be taken touching the sale of his maᵗⁱᵉˢ pepp &amp; haue examined the meanes ppounded by yoʳ LL: [(]being pleased to forbeare to put in vre his maᵗⁱᵉˢ pʳrogatiue) how the said meanes will sorte wᵗʰ the sale of his maᵗⁱᵉˢ pepp &amp; vpholdinge of the Companie.  Wᶜʰ we finde must eyther stand or fall by such a moderac̃on as is to be carried herein, and fallinge into Considerac̃on of the first offer of favoʳ to the said Companie mencioned in yoʳ lᵖˢ Lres wᶜʰ was that a ioynct sale of his maᵗⁱᵉˢ pepp wᵗʰ a like quantitie of the Companies pepp should be vttered and sold to gether equallie &amp; not otherwyse.  Wee finde in the Examinãcõn of that Course theis pticuler ympedimᵗᵉ followinge first agreate quantitie of the Companies pepp alredy disposed into many hande by a genʳal diuident agreed vpon amongst them selues before yoʳ ll: łres came to oʳ hande, beside other great quantities of pepp brought out of the lowe Countries and both of them soe passed from hand to hand from the mʳchaunt to the Grocer &amp; from the Grocer to the Chapman in the Countrey that by this meanes there is sufficient pepp alreadie deliuʳed out abroade in the Cittie and Countrie, to forbeare any further sale for one yeare att the least, soe as the vent of his maᵗⁱᵉˢ pepp by a ioynt retayling sale wᵗʰ the remaynder of the Companies pepp cann noe way advaunce any some of money in Convenient tyme to his maᵗⁱᵉˢ vse, worthie the attending the Event of this course.  Wᶜʰ being soe then their resteth onelie the other meane wᶜʰ is the sale of the kinge pepp to the Companie, <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-137" data-page="137" data-folio="45" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n136_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 45">[p. 45]</button> wherein we they Comitties on the said Companies behalfe doe humblie informe yo<sup>r</sup> ℔ that the said Companie haue vpon their hande alredie soe greate a masse of pepp, that allbeyt eu<sup>r</sup>y Adventurer thereof hath free libtie for the bringing in of 100<sup>li</sup> of money to take out vC<sup>li</sup> worth of pepper vpon his stocke to set forth a new voyadge to the East Indies to fetch home the remaynder of their goode there yet in reguard there is noe present sale thereof there is such drawing backe in pformeinge of this supplie that the voyadg hath not that expedi<span class="sc">c</span>õn w<sup>ch</sup> weare Convenient. Wherevpon we thinke that soe many pticuler men of soe diuers condi<span class="sc">c</span>õn will hardlie be drawen to ingage them selues for their seu<sup>r</sup>all propor<span class="sc">c</span>õns of their adventures to inlardg anie furder somes to buie a Comoditie that is like to lie soe longe vpon their hande. Vpon the Considera<span class="sc">c</span>õn of w<sup>ch</sup> difficultie, and the vnlikeliehood that the Generallitie of the Companie will be drawen to enterteyne this bargaine, we thought yt not meete to call the Companie together to propound this mo<span class="sc">c</span>õn vnto them, least yt should not onelie receiue a deniall, but being a matter putt into soe many distasted humo<sup>rs</sup> would be hardlie receiued and spoken off. We therefore seing noe present course to be followed that would bringe forth either benefitt to his ma<sup>tie</sup> or the Ccmpany, though[t] ffitt to shewe to yo<sup>r</sup> hono<sup>rs</sup> vpon what ympedim<sup>te</sup> we rest, desyreing to be furder directed herein. And we the Comitties for the Company doe humblie adu<sup>r</sup>tise yo<sup>r</sup> ℔ that yf the prepara<span class="sc">c</span>õn of the voyadge be any way crossed by vrging the gen<sup>r</sup>alitie w<sup>th</sup> further supplies of chardge, or hindring them in the vtteringe of their pepp diuided &amp; to be devided amongst them before the deptinge of the ffleete w<sup>ch</sup> will be aboute the end of ffebruarie, the voyadge will whollie be ou<sup>r</sup>throwne w<sup>ch</sup> may by yo<sup>r</sup> Lo<sup>ps</sup> ffavo<sup>r</sup> be vphoulded both to the benefitt of the kinge and the good of the Comon wealth by this small tollera<span class="sc">c</span>õn &amp; the shipps being once sent away the Company will be redie in all dutie to submitt them selues to any Course that shall seeme beneficiall to his ma<sup>tie</sup> for the venting of his pepp. And thus humblie resting at yo<sup>r</sup> ll Comaundem<sup>t</sup> &amp; service we take our leaue London the 8<sup>th</sup> of Decemb̃ 1603.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> Lo<sup>ps</sup> humble att Comaund</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">W<sup>m</sup> Rider</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Thomas Smyth gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-138" data-page="138" data-folio="46" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n137_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 46">[p. 46]</button> Tho: Middleton		W<sup>m</sup> Romeny<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-048" href="#sec-04-fn-048" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-048" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Samu: Saltonested	Tho: Bramley dep: Jo: Wolstonholme<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-049" href="#sec-04-fn-049" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-049" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup>	Tho: Cordall.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A lre from his matie to the kinge of Bantam in the East Indies by S<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-050" href="#sec-04-fn-050" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-050" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup></aside></div><p class="prose-p">JAMES by the grace of God kinge of England Scotland ffraunce and Ireland defendo<sup>r</sup> of the faith &amp;c: To the greate and mightie kinge of Bantam, &amp; of the dominions &amp; territories adioyning greetinge. Whereas the right of inheritaunce &amp; possession of theis o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes of England ffraunce &amp; Ireland by the decease of o<sup>r</sup> late deare Sister of ffamous memory is discended vpon vs, &amp; ioyned to o<sup>r</sup> other principalities &amp; kingdomes w<sup>ch</sup> we form<sup>r</sup>lie enioyed, we being established &amp; setled in the possession thereof haue received into <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-139" data-page="139" data-folio="47" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n138_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 47">[p. 47]</button> o<sup>r</sup> hande vpon the retourne of S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster &amp; other of o<sup>r</sup> subiecte from yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>ties</sup> kingdomes &amp; territories w<sup>th</sup> their shipps &amp; marchandize, not onelie yo<sup>r</sup> Princelie łres directed and sent to o<sup>r</sup> said deceased Sister, but alsoe yo<sup>r</sup> kinde present w<sup>ch</sup> did accompanie the same holding o<sup>r</sup> selfe after her deaceased inter-ressed therein by the right of o<sup>r</sup> Crowne &amp; Septer. Vpon the pvsall of w<sup>ch</sup> łres we weare possessed w<sup>th</sup> noe small ioye, that yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> had soe gratiouslie accepted the Comeing of o<sup>r</sup> subiecte into yo<sup>r</sup> dominions and kingdomes &amp; soe fauorablie and Royallie delt w<sup>th</sup> them whilest they aboade &amp; Contynued theare in traffique w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> subiecte: Of all w<sup>ch</sup> ffavors &amp; other yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>ties</sup> princelie pceedinge w<sup>th</sup> them o<sup>r</sup> said subiecte, S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster hath made vnto vs verie lardge &amp; ample relac̃on. This Introducc̃on being made into a mutuall amitie &amp; entercourse betweene yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> &amp; vs, we are desirous to Nourrish &amp; Contynue the same by all good meanes and oportunities that may be thought vpon or conceiued on o<sup>r</sup> pte. And to that end we haue geven lycence to diu<sup>r</sup>s of o<sup>r</sup> subiecte to prepare &amp; sett forth a new voyadge towarde yo<sup>r</sup> Countries and kingdomes, amongest w<sup>ch</sup> manie of them w<sup>ch</sup> were in the former voyadge are desirous for the good &amp; honorable vsadge they formerlie founde by yo<sup>r</sup> princelie favo<sup>r</sup> to visitt the same againe: others vpon their reporte are encouradged, notw<sup>th</sup>standing the longe &amp; daungerous Nauigac̃on to see the state &amp; manner of behavio<sup>r</sup> of people in Countries farr remote. Theis Considerac̃ons moueing both vs &amp; o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte to visitt yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>ties</sup> Countries with lawfull &amp; peaceable traffique of m<sup>r</sup>chandize, we doubt not but your ma<sup>tie</sup> will be well pleased therew<sup>th</sup>, &amp; not onelie soe, but w<sup>th</sup> the Continuanc[e] of an entercourse from yeare to yeare of o<sup>r</sup> m<sup>r</sup>chaunte w<sup>th</sup> their shipps &amp; goode into yo<sup>r</sup> kingdomes; whome we dare be bold to Com̃end to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-140" data-page="140" data-folio="48" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n139_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 48">[p. 48]</button> yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> for a people Civill and iust in their dealeing and trade &amp; euery way as able to furnish yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> &amp; yo<sup>r</sup> people w<sup>th</sup> all such C omodities w<sup>ch</sup> this parte of the world doth yeald, or affordeth as any Nac̃õn or people whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> heretofore haue made trade or traffique w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> people. And because a Capitulac̃õn &amp; establishing of Amitie, [and] entercourse to be contynued betweene yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> and vs cannot convenientlie be p̄formed by discourse of łres o<sup>r</sup> seu<sup>r</sup>all kingdomes being so farr distant &amp; remote thone from the other. We therefore pray yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> to giue eare therein to this bearer &amp; to giue him Creditt in whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> he shall vndertake or promyse in o<sup>r</sup> name concerninge the same w<sup>ch</sup> we promyse for o<sup>r</sup> p̄te in the worde of a prince shall be p̄formed, and will be redie gratefullie to requite any loue kindnes or favo<sup>r</sup> that o<sup>r</sup> said Subiectę shall receiue at yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>ties</sup> handę. And in token of o<sup>r</sup> Princelie loue to yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup>, we send you by this bearer a remembraunce &amp; kindnes, w<sup>ch</sup> we pray you to accept as from one that wisheth vnto yo<sup>u</sup> all good successe, &amp; happines. And soe Comend yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> to the tuic̃õn of the most highe God.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Note that the kinge writeth him not brother. And that another łre was now alsoe written from the kinge to some other Prince in those p̄tę of The teno<sup>r</sup> of that form<sup>r</sup>lie from Queene Elizabeth.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-051" href="#sec-04-fn-051" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-051" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Comission from his matie to Sr Henry Middleton</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Iames</span> by the grace of God kinge of England Scotland ffraunce and Ireland defendo<sup>r</sup> of the faith &amp;c: Trustie and welbeloued Henrie Middleton Esquier &amp; to o<sup>r</sup> trustie and welbeloued xpofer Colthurst<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-052" href="#sec-04-fn-052" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-052" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Gentleman greetinge. Wheareas diuers of o<sup>r</sup> loueing subiectę att their owne Adventures Costes and chardges aswell for the hono<sup>r</sup> of this o<sup>r</sup> Realme of England as for the encrease and advauncem<sup>t</sup> of trade &amp; m<sup>r</sup>chandize <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-141" data-page="141" data-folio="49" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n140_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 49">[p. 49]</button> w<sup>th</sup>in the same did heeretoforℯ sett forth a voyadge to the East Indies w<sup>th</sup> c<sup>r</sup>teyne shipps and Pynnacℯ by way of marchandizeinge, by w<sup>ch</sup> shipps &amp; Pynnacℯ they haueing discou<sup>r</sup>ed and begun to settle a trade in some pte of the said East Indies. And their shipps being retourned from thence laden w<sup>th</sup> sundrie marchandize brought from those partes they the said m<sup>r</sup>chauntℯ intendinge to frequent those Countries of the East Indies by Continuing the trade alredie discou<sup>r</sup>ed and begon &amp; endevo<sup>r</sup>inge further discou<sup>r</sup>y of trade of those partℯ for the more ample vent of the Natiue Comodities of o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes, &amp; retourninge from the places of their discou<sup>r</sup>y of such necessarie Comodities as shall be of spetiall vse &amp; benefitt both to vs &amp; o<sup>r</sup> subiectℯ are in hand to prepare and make redie their said shipps latelie retourned from the East Indies &amp; and to sett them forth againe for a new voyadge. And wheareas the said m<sup>r</sup>chauntℯ haue chosen you the said Henrie Middleton to be principall Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup>, or Generall, and you the said x̄pofer Colthurst to be Leiuetenaunte Generall of all the m<sup>r</sup>chauntℯ Marriners and other o<sup>r</sup> subiectℯ w<sup>ch</sup> are or shall be shipped in any of the said shipps. We gratiouslie favo<sup>r</sup>ing their intended voyadge, &amp; approueing &amp; allowinge of their choyse of you to the said Gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>te</sup> being desierous to furnish you, w<sup>th</sup> all ffitt &amp; Convenient power &amp; aucthoritie to rule &amp; Gou<sup>r</sup>ne all &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y o<sup>r</sup> subiectℯ imployed in this voyadge by a due obedience to be by them yealded, vnto you in obserueing &amp; executeinge of all such good orders &amp; Constitucõns as you shall thinke Convenient to ordeine &amp; appointe for the ffurtheraunce of the said voyadge to the hono<sup>r</sup> of vs and o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes, &amp; for the advauncem<sup>t</sup> of the said trade. We doe heereby straightlie chardge and Comaund all &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y pson &amp; psons ymployed vsed or shipped in this voyadge, in any of the said ships, to giue all due obedience &amp; respect vnto you dureing the said voyadge, &amp; to beare them selues therein one towardes another in all good order &amp; quietnes for avoideing any occasion that might breede Mutinie, quarrells or discention amongest them to the hindraunce of the good suckcess w<sup>ch</sup> is to be hoped for by Godℯ prouidence of the said intended voyadge, &amp; in default of such dutie and obedience to be pformed towardes you &amp; for the correccõn &amp; quenching of all such Mutenie quarrells or dissention, that shall or maye growe or be moued by the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-142" data-page="142" data-folio="50" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n141_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 50">[p. 50]</button> disorder, euill dispoticõn or pversnes of any of the said psons. We doe heereby aucthorize you Henrie Middleton Gen<sup>r</sup>all dureing the said voyadge or dureing soe longe tyme as you shall liue in the said voyadge. And in Case of yo<sup>r</sup> decease w<sup>ch</sup> God forbid, we doe then likewyse hereby aucthorize you the said Christopher Colthurst to chastice correct and ponnish all Offenders and Transgresso<sup>rs</sup> in that behalfe according to the qualitie of their Offences w<sup>th</sup> such ponishm<sup>t</sup> as are Comonlie vsed in all Armyes by Sea when the offences are not capitall. And for Capitall Offences as wilfull murder w<sup>ch</sup> is hatefull in the sight of God, or mutenie w<sup>ch</sup> is an offence that maie tend to the ou<sup>r</sup>throue of the said voyadge, the same being trulie &amp; iustlie proued agaynst any of the pson or psons aforesaid. We doe hereby giue vnto you the said Henrie Middleton during all the tyme of the said voyadge, or dureing soe long tyme as you shall liue in the said voyadge. And in Case of yo<sup>r</sup> decease we doe giue to you Christopher Colthurst full power and aucthoritie to vse and putt in execution o<sup>r</sup> lawe called Martiall in that behalfe and theis o<sup>r</sup> lres shall be vnto you sufficient warrante and dischargde for the doing &amp; executeinge of all &amp; singuler the p<sup>r</sup>misses. And forasmuch as at this present tyme wee are in Amitie w<sup>th</sup> all xptian People &amp; are vnwilling that any of o<sup>r</sup> subiecte should giue occasion of breach or hindraunce of that league or Amitie w<sup>ch</sup> we hold w<sup>th</sup> any o<sup>r</sup> Confederate ffreinde or Allies and because we are not ignorant of the Emulačõn and Envie that doth accompany the discou<sup>r</sup>y of Countries and trades, &amp; of the quarrells and contenčõns w<sup>ch</sup> doe many tymes fall out betweene the subiecte of diu<sup>r</sup>s Princes y<sup>t</sup> meete the one w<sup>th</sup> the other in fforraine and ffarr remote Countries, in the Course &amp; psecuteinge of their discou<sup>r</sup>ies, and being desirous y<sup>t</sup> o<sup>r</sup> subiecte should forbeare to moue or begin any quarrell or contenčõn vpon the subiecte of o<sup>r</sup> Confederate ffreinde or Allies either in the pceedinge or retourne vpon or from any of their voyage. Wee therefore doe hereby straightlie chardge &amp; Comaund you Henrie Middleton &amp; you xptofor Colthurst, &amp; all others vnder yo<sup>r</sup> Gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup>, that neither in your voyadg outwarde or homeward nor in any Countrie Iland Porte or place where you shall abide or come during the tyme of yo<sup>r</sup> being abroade out of o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes or dominions where you <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-143" data-page="143" data-folio="51" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n142_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 51">[p. 51]</button> may meete w<sup>th</sup> any the subiecte of the kinge of Spaine or of any other of o<sup>r</sup> Confederate ffreinde or Allies, their shipps vessells goode &amp; m<sup>r</sup>chandize, you doe attempt or goe aboute to sett vpon take or surprise their psons shipps vessells goode or m<sup>r</sup>chandize or offer any iniurie or discourtesie vnto them, vnlesse you shall first by them thearevnto iustlie be pvoked or driuen in the iust defence of yo<sup>r</sup> owne psons, yo<sup>r</sup> shipps vessells goode or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes as you will Aunswere to the Contrarie at yo<sup>r</sup> vttermost pills. In witnes whereof we haue caused theis o<sup>r</sup> łres to be made Pattente witnes o<sup>r</sup>selfe att Westminster the 3 daie of March in the first yeare of o<sup>r</sup> Reigne of England ffraunce &amp; Ireland &amp; of Scotland the Seauen &amp; Thirteth.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">A Comission</span> sett downe by vs the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> the Deputie &amp; Comitties of the East India Companie to o<sup>r</sup> loueing ffreinde m<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton Generall of the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte marrino<sup>rs</sup> &amp; others ymployed by vs in this present intended voyadge, being o<sup>r</sup> second voyadge to the East Indies &amp; to m<sup>r</sup> xptofer Colthurst, m<sup>r</sup> Roger Style<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-053" href="#sec-04-fn-053" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-053" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup>, m<sup>r</sup> Wittm Kealinge<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-054" href="#sec-04-fn-054" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-054" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> &amp; m<sup>rs</sup> o<sup>r</sup> principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunte and ffacto<sup>rs</sup> in the said voyadge, &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>r of them respectiuelie, as the direccons &amp; instruccons of this o<sup>r</sup> p<sup>r</sup>sent Comission maye Concerne their seu<sup>r</sup>all places of trust wherein the are ymployed the w<sup>ch</sup> voyadge Almightie god in his mercie make prosperous.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Wheareas</span> we haue vpon a spetiall conceite of yo<sup>r</sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-144" data-page="144" data-folio="52" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n143_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 52">[p. 52]</button> wysedome, discrecõn &amp; good gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup>, made choyse of you m<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton to be principall Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Generall of o<sup>r</sup> ffleete &amp; haue submitted to yo<sup>r</sup> Comaund the ꝑsons of all the marchaunte Marriners &amp; others ymployed in the seu<sup>r</sup>all shipps thereof w<sup>ch</sup> shipps we haue fitted and furnished w<sup>th</sup> all necessaries not onelie meete for the voyadge but such as you could require or wish to be supplied w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> full Contentm<sup>t</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-055" href="#sec-04-fn-055" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-055" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> The Compa: inducem<sup>t</sup> to make choise of theis psons.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">WEE therefore expect on yo<sup>r</sup> parte, such a ꝑformaunce &amp; carrying of this Gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>te</sup> Comitted to yo<sup>r</sup> Chardge as may not onelie Confirme vs in that hope and good concipt w<sup>ch</sup> induced vs to the choyse of yo<sup>u</sup> to this ymploym<sup>t</sup> but may add vnto yo<sup>r</sup> selfe an encrease &amp; advancem<sup>t</sup> of yo<sup>r</sup> owne reputacõn, w<sup>ch</sup> noe doubt you may effect, obserueing this moderacõn in yo<sup>r</sup> said Gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>te</sup> soe to Comaund as you may be both loued and feared, not vsing aucthoritie to worke yo<sup>r</sup> private respect or revenge but studieing and endevo<sup>r</sup>inge to bringe this longe and Tedious voyadge to a ꝑfitable end, w<sup>th</sup> care of the saffetie, health &amp; Comforte of yo<sup>r</sup> people &amp; vsing yo<sup>r</sup> industrie to recompence soe greate a Chardge of ꝑvisions and other burthens and expences borne in this voyadge w<sup>th</sup> a ꝑfitable retourne to the gen<sup>r</sup>all state of the Companie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-056-2" href="#sec-04-fn-056" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-056" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> To vse the assistaunce of c<sup>r</sup>taine nominated.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AND that you may the better ꝑceede in an vnfallable hope of agood yssue of yo<sup>r</sup> endevo<sup>rs</sup> we wish and exhorte you first to depend Confidentlie vpon Godes ꝑvidence, &amp; next ꝑpound to yo<sup>r</sup> selfe the good example of yo<sup>r</sup> late p<sup>r</sup>dicesso<sup>r</sup> S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster in the carryinge of the former voyadge. And forasmuch as noe man is soe absolute in his Course and direccõns for the manageing of any occasions of ymportaunce, but he May therein receiue light &amp; espetiall helpe and furtheraunce by Conference w<sup>th</sup> others, Therefore we haue for yo<sup>r</sup> Assistaunce &amp; helpe the better to vndergoe the Chardge of o<sup>r</sup> busines made choyse of m<sup>r</sup> x̃ptofer Colthurst, m<sup>r</sup> Roger Style, m<sup>r</sup> Will̃m Keeleing, m<sup>r</sup> Robert Browne<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-055-2" href="#sec-04-fn-055" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-055" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> &amp; m<sup>r</sup> Edward Highlord,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-056-3" href="#sec-04-fn-056" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-056" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-145" data-page="145" data-folio="53" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n144_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 53">[p. 53]</button> as o<sup>r</sup> principall m<sup>r</sup>chauntę ymployed in this voyadge to be readie w<sup>th</sup> their advice &amp; aide to assist you in any thinge that may belonge to the same whome we pray and requier you not onelie to heare but loueinglie &amp; kindlie to vse and respect soe as by yo<sup>r</sup> kinde Conversacõn mutuallie vsed &amp; had the one w<sup>th</sup> the other there maye growe betweene yo<sup>u</sup> in soe firme a vnitie as shall be subiect to noe Iarr or distaste wherein yf you linke together, all thinge will passe w<sup>th</sup> felicitie and Contentm<sup>t</sup> otherwyse yf there fall amongst you Envie emulacõn or disagreem<sup>t</sup> there is noe hope of good successe of the voyadge but apparant hazard to the ou<sup>r</sup>- throw of the same. &amp; this shall suffice for o<sup>r</sup> generall advise vnto you for such a moderacõn of gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>te</sup> to be pformed on yo<sup>r</sup> pte as may breede a good affeccõn towardes you in those that are submitted vnto yo<sup>r</sup> Com̃aund.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><sup>3</sup> Dalie prayer to be vsed by the wholle Companie.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AND to thend that the wholle Companie Comitted to yo<sup>r</sup> chardg may pforme that due obedience &amp; respect vnto you w<sup>ch</sup> is ffitt to be yealded to their Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> or Generall, we doe herein ppound vnto you the care of the due execution of that principall meane w<sup>ch</sup> draweth all Christians to Conformitie and submission to such as are set ou<sup>r</sup> them w<sup>ch</sup> is the dayly invocacõn &amp; religious wo<sup>p</sup>: &amp; service of God, requiring you to take order that c<sup>r</sup>teine houres &amp; tymes in eu<sup>r</sup>y day may be sett aparte for publique prayer &amp; calling vpon the name of God, that like orders w<sup>th</sup> penalties to be seu<sup>r</sup>elie published &amp; sett vpp in eu<sup>r</sup>y shipp agaynst the blasphemeing of the name of god, &amp; all Idle and filthie Comunicacõn: That all vnlawfull gameinge especialie dice play may be abolished as that w<sup>ch</sup> pcureth not onelie the blasphemeing of the name of God, but Envie and quarrellinge frome whence many tymes pceedeth murders or att the least the impou<sup>r</sup>ishinge and vndoinge of many of the poore vngou<sup>r</sup>ned Marriners, who by the lib̃tie of dice playe loose their whole wages of the voyadge as yt is not vnknowne vnto you, by the practise of diuers in the last voyadge who to furnish them selues w<sup>th</sup> money in that vnthrifftie ymploym<sup>t</sup> ingaged them selues to pay 3 for 1 vpon their retourne, &amp; thus haueing wasted <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-146" data-page="146" data-folio="54" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n145_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 54">[p. 54]</button> their wage by such vnthriftie meanes went aboute to laye a scandall vpon the Companie, alledgeing they were oppressed by necessitie in the voyadge to enter into those excessiue vsurious Contente<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-057" href="#sec-04-fn-057" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-057" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> to mayntayne their liues.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><sup>4</sup> To keepe Comp: one with another.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><sup>5</sup> Warlike guard to be kept in watering or refreshing men &amp;c:</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">You</span> haueing thus sett an order to be obserued in eu<sup>r</sup>y shipp both for the service of God, &amp; the Civill behavior of the Company amongest them selues, then for the better Assistaunce strength &amp; Comfort that the whole ffleete may take one of another by keeping Company together through out the wholle voyadge vntill you come into the East Indies into the porte or Haven of Bantam, where you are to take order w<sup>th</sup> the m<sup>rs</sup> &amp; Companie of eu<sup>r</sup>y shipp by the best observac̃ons &amp; direccons w<sup>ch</sup> you shall in yo<sup>r</sup> Conference together agree vpon that you depte not out of sight one of another soe longe as you may possiblie keepe together to thend that yf any of you should be any way distressed vpon any occasion you may be releued and Comforted by the Assistaunce of some of yo<sup>r</sup> Consorte not being farr of. And yf you shall happen to be sepated by ffowle weather &amp; cannot recouer the sight one of another in shorte tyme, yf yo<sup>r</sup> separac̃on or dispersinge happen to be before you come to Iland of May<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-058" href="#sec-04-fn-058" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-058" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> then lett that Iland be the place of yo<sup>r</sup> repaier or rendevous, that you may be drawen agayne together in Companie &amp; there to staie one for an other 7 or 8 daies, &amp; haueing made staie there soe longe &amp; the Company missing, not being Come thither, then to depte forwardes on the voyadge, leaueing some apparent marke behinde you that you haue beene at the said Iland, as by raysing some heape of Stones together, setting vpp of a stake or marke and leaueing there <em>some lre</em> in or neare the place w<sup>ch</sup> may be found by them as shall come after, whereby they may knowe that the other shipp or shipps are past that place. And yf yo<sup>r</sup> seperac̃on be beyond the Iland of May then yo<sup>r</sup> Rendevous to be att the Iland of St Lawrence. And yf you shall be occasioned to land in any place for fresh water, or to refresh yo<sup>r</sup> men yt shall behoue you to keepe good order, &amp; discipline by warlike guarde for the saffetie of your Companie least they should be surprized and fall into daunger by ou<sup>r</sup> much Confidence and <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-147" data-page="147" data-folio="55" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n146_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 55">[p. 55]</button> securitie &amp; the saffetie of yo<sup>r</sup> ꝑsons being ꝑvided for then yo<sup>r</sup> wholle Companie to be admonished to behaue them selues peaceably &amp; Civillie towardes the people of that place, where you refresh for the supplie of yo<sup>r</sup> wantę, &amp; that they be alsoe exhorted to a moderac̃on in feeding of fruitę &amp; fresh victualls of that soyle, the w<sup>ch</sup> by their intemperate &amp; vnmoderate dyet, may breede inconveniences formerlie found by experience to be the losse of many mens liues. And in such place or places of refreshing you shall doe well to giue spetiall order to some men chosen &amp; appointed to that end, to make the gen<sup>r</sup>all ꝑvision bot of ffresh victualls &amp; fruite for the whole ffleete whereby an equall repartition thereof may be made for the Company of eu<sup>r</sup>y shipp and that eu<sup>r</sup>y one be not w<sup>th</sup>[out] lymitac̃on, to victuall hymselfe according to his intemperate appetite, and this order to be taken ꝑhibiting eu<sup>r</sup>y ꝑson vpon payne of seveare ponishm<sup>te</sup> that they doe not raunge and straggle after fresh victualls and fruitę but by such order as shall be p<sup>r</sup>scribed vnto them.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">6 refreshing by landing not to be vsed vpon light occasion.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">VPPON such oportunities of necessarie landinge of yo<sup>r</sup> people for refreshinge, w<sup>ch</sup> by noe meanes we would haue otherwyse to be vsed vpon light occasion for that yt will greatlie hinder the voyadge wee doe requier you to giue order vnto the preacher to prepare him selfe to preach to the people being Come together out of the seu<sup>r</sup>all shipps, makeing his choyse of such ffitt argum<sup>te</sup> &amp; places of scripture as may be most agreable to the tyme and occasion whereby the whole Company may be exhorted and taught the better to carrie themselues in the gen<sup>r</sup>all busynes. And that the preacher may haue the more Comforte in his mynistry, we pray you be carefull that all due respectę be geven him, not onelie by yo<sup>r</sup> selfe but by the wholle Company that his doctrine &amp; exhortac̃ons by Contempt or neglect of his mynistrye retourne not w<sup>th</sup>out proffitt. for the place of yo<sup>r</sup> refreshinge we wish yt to be the Iland of St Lawrence, but not at Saldania in any wyse for the inconveniences of that Iland noted vnto vs by men of good experience, &amp; their causion geven vs to be ware of the daunger of that place wherefore we requier you to shun this place as o<sup>r</sup> expresse order &amp; will herein.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">7 To goe thence to Bantam &amp; to dispose of busines.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">YOUR refreshing place being left &amp; all oportunities taken <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-148" data-page="148" data-folio="56" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n147_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 56">[p. 56]</button> for thadmonishing of yo<sup>r</sup> people both to the service of God w<sup>th</sup>out w<sup>ch</sup> noe enterprize cann be prosperous and to the Civill &amp; orderlie carryinge of them selues in the voyadge, &amp; in all service thereto belongeinge &gt; they<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-059" href="#sec-04-fn-059" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-059" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> you are to shape yo<sup>r</sup> Course directlie for Bantam aforesaid. Att w<sup>ch</sup> porte as soone as you shall arriue &amp; that you may Convenientlie drawe yo<sup>r</sup> selues together in Conference w<sup>th</sup> m<sup>r</sup> Willm Starkey [or] the agent w<sup>ch</sup> you shall finde theare succeedinge him in the busines. Then we doe requier you as god hath guided &amp; brought you to yo<sup>r</sup> expected &amp; appointed porte, where you may enter into the disposeing of yo<sup>r</sup> busines, that then you being Come to the m<sup>r</sup>chaundizeing porte of the voyadge you doe pceede therein as followeth. Viz.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">8 To advice and Conferr, of y<sup>e</sup> state of the place: vsadge &amp; all other Circumstances.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">9 To lande moneys</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">10 Garblers sent</aside></div><p class="prose-p">WEE DOE will and require you m<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton o<sup>r</sup> Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> or Gen<sup>r</sup>all of the whole voyadge, &amp; you m<sup>r</sup> xptofer Colthurst, m<sup>r</sup> Roger Style, m<sup>r</sup> Willm Kealinge, m<sup>r</sup> Robt Browne &amp; m<sup>r</sup> Edward Highlorde o<sup>r</sup> principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunt<em>e</em> for the disposeing of the m<sup>r</sup>chandises &amp; traffique of yo<sup>r</sup> saide voyadge that you advise &amp; Conferr together w<sup>th</sup> m<sup>r</sup> w<sup>m</sup> Starkey, or w<sup>th</sup> whome soeu<sup>r</sup> standeth in the place of the Agent resident att Bantam of the state of the place &amp; of the vsadge that o<sup>r</sup> Agent and ffactor<sup>s</sup> haue receiued since they were left theare &amp; of all other Circumstaunces that may concerne the saffetie and benefitt of the trade, w<sup>ch</sup> being found to stand in good tearmes then you are to deliu<sup>r</sup> o<sup>r</sup> lres to o<sup>r</sup> said Agent &amp; w<sup>th</sup> his advise to deliu<sup>r</sup> the king<em>e</em> ma<sup>ties</sup> lres &amp; present to the kinge of that place, &amp; then to land all such moneys as are laden in o<sup>r</sup> 4 seu<sup>r</sup>all shipps viz 11160<sup>li</sup> 12<sup>s</sup> in Royalls of 8 whereof in the red dragon DRAGON ffiue Chest<em>e</em> Cont: 4000<sup>li</sup> in Royalls in 40 bagg<em>e</em> HECTOR, ffower Chest<em>e</em> cont: 3200. in Royalls in 32 bagg<em>e</em>. ASSENTION, three Chest<em>e</em> Cont: 2400 in Royalls in 24 bagg<em>e</em> SUZAN, twoe Chest<em>e</em> Cont: 1560⅔ in Royalls in 15 bagges. <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-149" data-page="149" data-folio="57" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n148_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 57">[p. 57]</button> w<sup>ch</sup> being done then to take informacon, from the Agent of the state of o<sup>r</sup> busines left in his chardge what quantetie of ladeing he hath in aredines for the shipps causing cawsing the dust to be sifte from the pepp, yf yt be not doene alredie vpod advise geuen [to] that end by o<sup>r</sup> former łres for w<sup>ch</sup> purpose we send w<sup>th</sup> you garblers w<sup>th</sup> siues and ffanns to thend that o<sup>r</sup> shipps should not be dischargded w<sup>th</sup> vnproffitable Comodities.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">II What Comodities fitt &amp; what prouided att the Molloccos.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">TO TAKE like informačon what Comodities &amp; how much ladinge is pvided att the Molloccos conferringe w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> said Agent what shipps of o<sup>r</sup> ffleete ar ffittest to goe for the Molloccos remembring that the Hector and Assenčon are victualled for 24 monnethes: the experience of o<sup>r</sup> said ffact[o<sup>rs</sup>] gayned by their longe aboade att Bantam will well informe you how to pceede in the sending of o<sup>r</sup> shippinge to the Molloccos and for the ordering of the m<sup>r</sup>chandize for that place.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">12 The ships laden &amp; sent from Bantam for England whoe is to goe to the Molloccos &amp; to what end.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">THE pvision for the ladeinge att Bantam being laden abourde o<sup>r</sup> shipps that are first to retourne for England &amp; the Comaunders thereof shipped in the said shipps and those shipps p<sup>r</sup>pared readie w<sup>ch</sup> by you &amp; the Agente shall be resolved vpon to goe for the Molloccos o<sup>r</sup> direcčon is that you m<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton to goe yo<sup>r</sup> selfe in one of the said shipps to the molloccos takeing w<sup>th</sup> you such of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors to leaue to reside in the Molloccos &amp; such stocke for their maynetaynaunce, as you shall vpon Conference together att Bantam agree vpon to remayne in their hande for the mayntenance of them &amp; the residue w<sup>ch</sup> are alredie Resident att Bantam by their aboade theare, in w<sup>ch</sup> space they may both learne the languadge of the Countrie, and dispatch such busines as they shall be by you appointed vnto: And we wish you to take w<sup>th</sup> you to Banda one or both of the garblers w<sup>th</sup> their instrm<sup>te</sup> and provisions to Clense the Cloues from dust and stalkes &amp;
