the Nutmegge from Rumps and dust that o<sup>r</sup> shipps be not pestered w<sup>th</sup> the dust or garble of theis kinde of Comodities.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">noe particuler trade &amp;c: pena<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-060" href="#sec-04-fn-060" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-060" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> losse of</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">the Commodities laden except &amp;c:</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AND for that the voyage of this Condičon &amp; greate chardge cannot admitt any priuate trade, o<sup>r</sup> will is that neither yo<sup>r</sup> selfe nor any others vpon any pticuler <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-150" data-page="150" data-folio="58" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n149_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 58">[p. 58]</button> or other Accompt then the gen<sup>r</sup>all and ioynct stocke doe lade or be pmitted to lade any of theis Cōmodities in o<sup>r</sup> shipps viz: pepp Cloues mace Nut muge China silke Indigo, Amber greece, muske, sivitt, Bezar stones Camphire, Beniamyn Burrace<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-061" href="#sec-04-fn-061" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-061" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> or Synamon, but that what quantitie soeu<sup>r</sup> of theis Cōmodities may be had or brought vpp shall be laden for the ioynt accompte of the Company vpon payne of the losse of eu<sup>r</sup>y such Cōmoditie soe laden, &amp; not laden vpon the ioynct Accompte. But yf vpon lycence demaunded of you the said Generall &amp; you o<sup>r</sup> said principall marchaunte any m<sup>r</sup>, marriner or others shall be desirous to lade some small pporcõn or quantitie of China dishes or light triffles not exceeding the value of Three pounde, or not beareing aboue the bulcke of a small chest Then we doe order that all such goode soe laden by yo<sup>r</sup> privitie &amp; lycence shall be entred into the Pursars Booke of such shipp wherein the same is laden to thend that yf any of them doe die by the waye their freinde may enioye that w<sup>ch</sup> is theirs according to their wills.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">what goodes left in theast Indies since bought &amp;c what moneys &amp; debtes &amp;c:</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> for the better remembraunce &amp; instruccõn of you the said Gen<sup>r</sup>all &amp; of you the principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunte what goode were left behinde at Bantam, in the Comeing away of S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster out of the Indies &amp; what hath beene puided since by the Agent there as by their advice sent vs since the comeing home of o<sup>r</sup> shipps maye appeare. You shall vnderstand That S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster left att Bantā aboue 1500 bagge of pepp &amp; by their łres sent from Bantam by the dutch shipps the agent wrote that he had puided 1500 bagge more. And after S<sup>r</sup> Iames gaue Comission to the Agent at his depture from Bantam that yf the dutchmen were disposed to buie any of the Companies pepp &amp; and would giue good pffitt for yt that then the Agent should sell yt &amp; pvide more for the Companie att better oportunitie &amp; better rate vpon w<sup>ch</sup> Comission soe left you maye inquire what hath beene done for the benefitt of the Companie.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-151"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="151" data-folio="59" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n150_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 59">[p. 59]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Itt</span> doth alsoe appeare by the abbreuiate of the accompte sent home out of the Indies that there remayned in the hande of the Agent m<sup>r</sup> Starkey 482 ffardells of Callicoes viz 8 Canisters of Pinthadoes<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-062" href="#sec-04-fn-062" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-062" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> &amp; 117 ffardells of Checkered stuffes 51 ffardells of long malow girdles<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-063" href="#sec-04-fn-063" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-063" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> 59 ffs of girdles for <em>Sysan</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-064" href="#sec-04-fn-064" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-064" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> 110 ffs of Iava girdles,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-065" href="#sec-04-fn-065" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-065" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> 13 Cheste of fine Pinthadoes 6 Cheste of diuers sorte of Comodities, 42 ffs of Browne Callicoes &amp; in loose Pinthadoes, &amp; in loose Pinthadoes by estimate made by S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster aboute 80 ffe att the least of all sorte soe <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-152" data-page="152" data-folio="60" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n151_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 60">[p. 60]</button> that there might be in all packe &amp; canister aboute 482 ſſe as aforesaid &amp; in loose Callicoes so[m]e 30 ſſe soe as there appereth in the whole to be 512 ſſe since the dꬴpture of our shipps we vnderstand by their łres of the 15<sup>th</sup> of ffebruarie 1602 they had shipped [52 ſſe] for Banda in the Molloccos abourd o<sup>r</sup> Pinnace<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-066" href="#sec-04-fn-066" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-066" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> w<sup>ch</sup> we trust arriued there in saffetie w<sup>th</sup>in shorte tyme after her dꬴpture from Bantam, vpon the pceede of w<sup>ch</sup> 52 ſſe yt is hoped by vs that there maie be sufficient w<sup>th</sup> a lardge ou<sup>r</sup>plus to lade the 2 shipps we here appointed to the Molloccos w<sup>th</sup> mace, Cloues, &amp; Nutmuge soe that there remayned after the dꬴpture of the Pinnace from Bantam 452<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-067" href="#sec-04-fn-067" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-067" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> ſſe or thereaboute Conteyneing the best &amp; richest Comodities of the whole Complem<sup>te</sup> of the prize the other 52 ſſe sent to the molloccos being of the meanest &amp; basest kinde of the said prize goode w<sup>ch</sup> greate remaynder of goode att Bantam of the best &amp; greatest value cannot but vpon the pceede thereof [make] readie before yo<sup>r</sup> arrivall att Bantam great quantities of Comodities to be retourned from thence.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The 11160 <sup>3</sup>⁄<sub>5</sub><sup>li</sup> 12<sup>s</sup> to be ymployed not in spices but in other Commodities mor proffitable.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">BESIDES all w<sup>ch</sup> seu<sup>r</sup>all quantities of goods before mencioned there remayned in the hande of m<sup>r</sup> Starkey &amp; the rest at the dꬴpture of the shipps homewarde 4907 Royalls of Eight &amp; in debte 3941. pece of 8 being Compassed in the same 77 bagge of pepp w<sup>ch</sup> pepp was rec<sup>d</sup> into their Custodie soe as yf all the goode &amp; money left w<sup>th</sup> them &amp; that w<sup>ch</sup> might &amp; hath pceeded thereof doe rest in saffetie you shall not onelie haue sufficient to lade yo<sup>r</sup> fower shipps w<sup>th</sup> pepp mace Cloues &amp; Nutmuge but as many more shipps of the like burthen beside the moneys w<sup>ch</sup> you carrie alonge w<sup>th</sup> you in the shipps amounteing to 11160 <sup>3</sup>⁄<sub>5</sub><sup>li</sup> 12<sup>s</sup> in Royalls as aforesaid, w<sup>ch</sup> moneys because yo<sup>u</sup> knowe that spices are heere of noe value we wish you to ymploy in some other Comodities that may be of more estimãcõn &amp; yeald a better pffitt as rawe silke well Chosed &amp; bought at resonable prices or such like Comodities wherew<sup>th</sup> theis parte of Christendome haue not beene gluted as w<sup>th</sup> spices.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">The 11160 <sup>3</sup>⁄<sub>5</sub><sup>li</sup> 12<sup>s</sup> not to be mixt in accompt</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">w<sup>th</sup> the former Adventure &amp; cause whie.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AND touching the said 11160 <sup>3</sup>⁄<sub>5</sub><sup>li</sup> in Royalls now sent in theis ships forsomuch as yt is a new supplie of stocke sent out vpon a new Aduenture, we will not haue <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-153" data-page="153" data-folio="61" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n152_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 61">[p. 61]</button> the same to be mixed in accompt w<sup>th</sup> the former adventure but doe requier you to keepe the accompt of the ymploym<sup>t</sup> hereof alone by y<sup>t</sup> selfe. And to the end that the same may be distinguished from the former accompte of the first Adventure we doe pray and requier you the said Gen<sup>r</sup>all and principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunte by Conference and assistaunce of the ffactor<sup>s</sup> resident both att Bantam &amp; Banda to make a valuacõn as neare as you cann of all the remaynder of the first stocke, what it may be worth to be sold, for that many of the Adventurers who are vnwilling to hange &amp; Contynue longe in accompte vnpfect &amp; vndetermined ar desirous to sell their remaynders of their stocke to such as will Continue the trade, and therefore a valuacõn is to be conceiued &amp; sent w<sup>th</sup> the retourne of theis shipps att any hand being a matter not onelie desiered by the gen<sup>r</sup>alitie but expreslie agreed vpon &amp; Chardged vpon vs that haue the ordering of the busines to see y<sup>t</sup> pformeđ, w<sup>ch</sup> chardge being not in vs to pforme we laye vpon you in whose power the pformaunce thereof lieth as you will aunsweare the neglect thereof.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">what m<sup>r</sup> Starkey hath besides the former goodes &amp;c: left att Bantam.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Besides</span> the former pticulers of prize goode &amp; moneys before mencioned there appeth further to rest in the hande of m<sup>r</sup> Starkey and the other ffactor<sup>s</sup> 10 peeces of Clothes of diuers coullor<sup>s</sup> Cont: 514 english yarde a bason &amp; Ewer of siluer poiz 102 oz, twoe standing Cupps poiz 63 oz whereof they are to giue you an accompt att yo<sup>r</sup> being att Bantam.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">What wood is to be prouided.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Iff</span> those pte of the Indies doe yeald any good quantitie of Ibony ffarnando bucke<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-068" href="#sec-04-fn-068" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-068" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> or such like heavie wood of value, you may pvide soe much thereof as will ballast yo<sup>r</sup> shipps soe that the same take vpp <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-154" data-page="154" data-folio="62" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n153_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 62">[p. 62]</button> noe roome of stowinge that maye otherwyse be more p̃fitablye ymployed.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To p̃rvent the Portingalls mallice &amp; what is to be carried to the Molloccos.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> forasmuch as we are not ygnoraunt that the mallice of the Portingalls towarde o<sup>r</sup> discou<sup>r</sup>y of the trade to those p̃tes will not lett him abstayne from all practices of annoyaunce w<sup>ch</sup> lieth in his power to offer and p̃forme to the trade of the Mollocos, who happilie<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-069" href="#sec-04-fn-069" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-069" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> maye by some layinge of waight for the intercepting of o<sup>r</sup> Penace sent to Banda depri[ue] vs of the p̃visions w<sup>ch</sup> otherwyse that voyadge might supply vs w<sup>th</sup>all yf any such or other p̃rventing accident hath happened: yet would we haue you to carrie in yo<sup>r</sup> twoe shipps appointed for the Molloccos such quantitie of Pinthadoes moneys &amp; other p̃visions as may suffice to lade the said twoe shipps.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">leaueing other thinges of the state of y<sup>e</sup> busines to Consideracon and the states &amp; healthes of men &amp;c:</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Thus</span> we haue touched many p̃ticulers of o<sup>r</sup> busines not w<sup>th</sup>standinge many other thinge may occur w<sup>ch</sup> may greatlie concerne vs, w<sup>ch</sup> we must leaue to yo<sup>r</sup> good Considerac̃ons to deale therein as time and occasion shall offer you occasion Comendinge vnto your care the state of such as shall fall sicke in the voyadge either outward or inward that they may be Comforted: And yf the die see that their goode may be kept in saffetie, and their wills &amp; dispotition thereof soe well testified that there growe not theke<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-070" href="#sec-04-fn-070" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-070" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> suspition of the truth of some of their wills as hath beene had of the wills of others that dyed in the last voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Anie dieing others to be placed as they were first appointed &amp; soe consequentlie &amp;c:</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">And</span> forasmuch as the daies ot mans lyfe are lymitted &amp; the c<sup>r</sup>taine lymitac̃on thereof onelie knowne vnto god, we doe heere by ordeyne &amp; puide, that whereas we haue appointed &amp; placed you the said Henrie Middleton in the Redd Dragon as Gen<sup>r</sup>all or gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of the wholle ffleete &amp; you m<sup>r</sup> xptofer Colthurst as leiuetenant gen<sup>r</sup>all and principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunt in the Hecto<sup>r</sup> &amp; you m<sup>r</sup> Roger Style o<sup>r</sup> principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunte in the Assenc̃on, &amp; you m<sup>r</sup> Will̃m Keeleinge another of o<sup>r</sup> principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunte in the Suzan: yf therfore yt shall happen any of you to decease in this voyadge we doe ordeyne that he that in place &amp; order by vs appointed to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-155" data-page="155" data-folio="63" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n154_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 63">[p. 63]</button> goe as principall in eu<sup>r</sup>y shipp w<sup>ch</sup> shall surviue the ptie deceased shall &amp; may by the appointem<sup>t</sup> of the Generall, shall<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-071" href="#sec-04-fn-071" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-071" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> him selfe in the shipp out of w<sup>ch</sup> the said ptie did decease, and hold his place in the same shipp and the place that the ptie deceased held gen<sup>r</sup>allie in the voyadge and soe a succession to be held from the generall to the Leiuetenaunte from the leiuetenaunte to the principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunt placed in the Assenc̃on from the principall m<sup>r</sup>chant of the Assenc̃on to the principall m<sup>r</sup>chant placed in the Suzan: And yf yt shall happen all the principalls of the seu<sup>r</sup>all shipps to decease (as god forbid[]) the like succession to holde &amp; be kept by the seu<sup>r</sup>all m<sup>r</sup>chaunte appointed to hold the second places in eu<sup>r</sup>y the said shipps &amp; soe in succession from one to another according as the seu<sup>r</sup>all m<sup>r</sup>chaunte hold place in eu<sup>r</sup>y shipp.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">wheare m<sup>r</sup> Starkey shall be shipped yf he retourne home.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">P<span class="sc">ROUIDED</span> alwayes that yf m<sup>r</sup> Wittm Starkey o<sup>r</sup> Agent resident att Bantam doe come home in the retourne of the shipps then we doe requier you o<sup>r</sup> generall &amp; principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunte to take espetiall order that he be pvided for &amp; placed in such shipp as he shall be shipped as a man that we hould to be had in good reguarde, &amp; to be respected accordinglie and yf any of the principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunte places fall void by the death of any of them, or otherwyse, that then he supplie that place in his retourne homewardes.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">m<sup>r</sup> Colthurst leauetenaunt to succeede m<sup>r</sup> Hen: Middleton gen<sup>r</sup>all yf he decease vnto whome Comaund of the men shall be as in the gen<sup>r</sup>all.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">A<span class="sc">ND</span> lastelie whereas his ma<sup>tie</sup> vnder his great seale of England hath appointed you the saied m<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton the generall and principall Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of all his subjecte ymployed in the voyadge &amp; you the saied m<sup>r</sup> xptofer Colthurst to be leiuetenaunt gen<sup>r</sup>all, And yf you the said xptofer Colthurst surviue the said Henrie Middleton then to succeed him in the place of Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> or Generall w<sup>th</sup>out appointing any further succession by like warrant to any that is ymployed in the said voyadge and that yt lyeth not in vs to giue to any of the residue of o<sup>r</sup> principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunte any warrant for the Correc̃on of Offences by penall lawes to be executed vpon the bodies of any his ma<sup>ties</sup> subiecte, we doe in that behalfe as to men haueing <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-156" data-page="156" data-folio="64" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n155_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 64">[p. 64]</button> reason and discrecõn &amp; to men that feare god offer vnto yo<sup>r</sup> Consideracõns the benefitt of order &amp; peaceable gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>te</sup> in matters &amp; enterprizes vndertaken for a Comon good, reposeing in you o<sup>r</sup> seuerall m<sup>r</sup>chauntę &amp; all you o<sup>r</sup> sev<sup>r</sup>all Officers appointed &amp; intertayned in this voyadge a spetiall hope trust and Confidence that you will accorde and agree together and ioyne in freindshipp &amp; amytie to doe &amp; execute yo<sup>r</sup> vttermost endevo<sup>rs</sup> for the benefitt of the voyadge w<sup>th</sup>out Contencõn discord or emulacõn to be vsed amongst you guideing yo<sup>r</sup>selues therein by that gen<sup>r</sup>all Regim<sup>t</sup> &amp; sea gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>te</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> o<sup>r</sup> English ffleete doe vse when they sorte them selues together, haueing espetiall &amp; due respect to him that is the principall or cape m<sup>r</sup>chante &amp; soe we Comend yo<sup>u</sup> and yo<sup>r</sup> endeuo<sup>rs</sup> to goode<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-072" href="#sec-04-fn-072" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-072" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> puidence who guide you w<sup>th</sup> his feare and defend you from all daungers Amen.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Post scriptū.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">vnto whome the power of the factors placing shalbe in &amp; o<sup>r</sup> placeing to be noe rule.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">As</span> touching such ffacto<sup>rs</sup> as are to be left in the Countrie or of those w<sup>ch</sup> goe now in theis shipps we doe agree and giue order by this o<sup>r</sup> Commission that the appointm<sup>t</sup> of the said ffacto<sup>rs</sup> to reside in those places shall be att the direccõns and Consent of you o<sup>r</sup> Generall, m<sup>r</sup> xptofer Colthurst, m<sup>r</sup> Starkey and m<sup>r</sup> Morgan or any three of you, wherein we hope you will haue that Consideracõn as to make choyse of the fittest: neither lett the placeing of o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> in the seu<sup>r</sup>all shipps as, they are alredie placed be any rule to you, or any ground for them to enioye those places of ymploym<sup>t</sup> wherein you are to bestowe them, otherwyse then they shall be found able &amp; meete to deserue that trust of ymploym<sup>te</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">what we were advised of out of the lowe Countries from Bantam since all thinges were in a redines.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">The</span> shipps being readie to depte &amp; all o<sup>r</sup> Comissions &amp; instruccõns being resolued vpon and finished: wee receiued łres out of the lowe Countries w<sup>ch</sup> came from o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> from the East Indies by the dutch shipps viz from m<sup>r</sup> Will<sup>m</sup> Starkey of the 22<sup>th</sup> of Iune and 2 other łres one from Edmond Scott and the other from Thomas Tudd both dated the 17<sup>th</sup> of August 1603. by w<sup>ch</sup> latter łres we were aduised of the death of m<sup>r</sup> Starkey and m<sup>r</sup> Morgan &amp; of others who were left att Bantam and alsoe of the damadge that <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-157" data-page="157" data-folio="65" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n156_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 65">[p. 65]</button> happened vnto vs in o<sup>r</sup> goode burnt by fyer in the dutch warehowse together w<sup>th</sup> the disappointem<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> intended voyadge to the Molloccos by the Contrarietie of wynde.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-073" href="#sec-04-fn-073" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-073" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> All w<sup>ch</sup> thinge being Considered we take them as they were indeede the hand of god, who disposeth of all enterprizes according to his good will &amp; pleasure, w<sup>ch</sup> Accidente howsoeu<sup>r</sup> by the death of o<sup>r</sup> said principall ffacto<sup>rs</sup> they doe in some manner moue vs to alter o<sup>r</sup> said Comissions &amp; advises soe farr as we had appointed any thinge to be done by the advise of the said m<sup>r</sup> Starkey &amp; m<sup>r</sup> Morgan, yet in substance we purpose to hold o<sup>r</sup> former resolucõns for the following of o<sup>r</sup> busines And in steede of the ꝑsons deceased we wysh you to advise &amp; vse Conference w<sup>th</sup> the ffacto<sup>rs</sup> remayneing, whome you finde most apt &amp; able to assist you in the direction of the busines and touching yo<sup>r</sup> pceedinge in the voyadge from Bantam to the Molloccos after order for lading of the Dragon and the Suzan w<sup>th</sup> pepp to retourne for England being taken we thinke y<sup>t</sup> fitt you Capt: Middleton p<sup>r</sup>pare yo<sup>r</sup>selfe w<sup>th</sup>all speede to goe for Banda and doe furnish yo<sup>r</sup> selfe w<sup>th</sup> the most aptest &amp; ffitest of o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> to attend you, and to be left att Banda, for the furder followinge of that trade according as yt may be found likelie to bring benefitt to the Companie wherein you are accordinge to yo<sup>r</sup> good discrecõn &amp; Consideracõn to vse the more or fewer ffacto<sup>rs</sup> to be left there as you shall finde the more or lesse hope of benefitt to be made by their residence in that place not forgetting the Companyes desire as neere as you cann possiblie to cleare the former voyadge according to o<sup>r</sup> former direccõn, by a discreete &amp; resonable valuacõn of the remayner. And for the accomplishing of ladeing att Banda in the Moloccos you o<sup>r</sup> said gen<sup>r</sup>all are to take w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> soe much of the Comodities remayneing in o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> hande att Bantam as they thinke will serue for the puiding of yo<sup>r</sup> ladinge att the Molloccos according to the pportion geuen by S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster to puide, remembring that they dutchmen bought of Spillesbe<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-074" href="#sec-04-fn-074" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-074" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> the like Comodities to carrie to the molloccos <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-158" data-page="158" data-folio="66" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n157_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 66">[p. 66]</button> for the better furnishing of their lading: but for yo<sup>r</sup> better assuraunce least the saide Comodities should not be in request there, you shall doe well to take w<sup>th</sup> you 10000, 15000, or 20000 Royalls of 8 as you shall thinke meete &amp; being soe furnished both by wares and money, you may both puide you ladinge and supplie yo<sup>r</sup> ffactors w<sup>ch</sup> you shall thinke ffitt to leaue to recyde theare w<sup>th</sup> sufficient stocke for puision of Cloues and Mace agaynst another yeare. This piect being lade downe for the trade att Banda you shall doe well in yo<sup>r</sup> going thither to touch att the Iland of Amboyna or any other Iland by the way where Cloues may be had and to furnish yo<sup>r</sup> selfe w<sup>th</sup> what quantitie you cann gett whereby you may the lesse depend vpon yo<sup>r</sup> puision of Cloues att Banda, &amp; haueing directed and ordered yo<sup>r</sup> busines for the Moloccos, then vpon yo<sup>r</sup> retourne to Bantam to take full and pfect knowledge of the wholle state of o<sup>r</sup> busines &amp; to leaue 10 or 12 ffactors there w<sup>th</sup> such stocke as shall remayne vnymployed or as may be spared o<sup>r</sup> shipps being laden admonishing the ffactors to be more carefull to buye their pepp att the best advantage then they haue beene, &amp; to buie of the largest pepp w<sup>ch</sup> heare beareth the best estimac̃on, and vnlesse you shall finde yt verie necessarie that some ffactors be left att Banda, we are of opinion &amp; doe like well that the residue of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors be holden altogether att Bantam but herein we leaue you to yo<sup>r</sup> owne experience, as yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde the Course most convenient And soe as before Comend you to godę puidence</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AND whereas o<sup>r</sup> ffactors deceased doe appeare to haue left some estatę behinde them w<sup>ch</sup> must haue meanes to come to the hande of such as haue interest therein we doe wysh you to take order: that all thingę that doe appeare to belonge vnto them be duelie and trulie inventoried &amp; shipped aparte by them selues, and their wills saffelie kepte and brought home together w<sup>th</sup> their bookę &amp; notę of their buieing and sellingę whereby there estates were encreased &amp; gotten, to thend that the Company may be satisfied that they haue beene well delt w<sup>th</sup>all therein &amp; their ffreinde receaue that w<sup>ch</sup> shall rightfullie appteine vnto them.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-159"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="159" data-folio="67" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n158_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 67">[p. 67]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">from the kinge of Tedora to his matie by Captaine Henrie Middleton deliured to his matie in Maye. 1606.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">This</span><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-075" href="#sec-04-fn-075" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-075" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> writeing of the kinge of Tydore to the kinge ma<sup>tie</sup> of England is to lett yo<sup>r</sup> highnes vnderstand that the kinge of Holland hath sent hither into theis partes, a ffleete of shipps to ioyne w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> auncient enymie the kinge of Turnato &amp; the ioyntelie together haue ouer runn and spoyled parte of o<sup>r</sup> Countrie &amp; are determined to distroy both vs and o<sup>r</sup> subiecte. Now vnderstanding by the bearer hereof Capt: Henrie Middleton that yo<sup>r</sup> highnes is in freindshipp, w<sup>th</sup> the kinge of Spaine we desire yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> that you would take pittie of vs that we may not be distroyed by the kinge of Holland, and Turnato to whome we haue offered noe wronge But they by forceable meanes seeke to bereaue vs of o<sup>r</sup> kingdome, and all greate kinge vpon the Earth are ordeyned by God to sucker all them that be wrongfullie oppressed soe I appeale vnto yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> for sucker agaynst my enimyes not doubting but to finde releefe att yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>ts</sup> hande. And yf yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> send hither I humblie intreate that yt may be Capt: Henrie Middleton or his brother w<sup>th</sup> whome I am well acquainted: Thus we end praying God to enlardge yo<sup>r</sup> kingdomes &amp; bless yo<sup>u</sup> &amp; all yo<sup>r</sup> Councells. Tydore.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">from the kinge of Bantam to or kinge wth a prsent of twoe Bezar stones by genrall Henr Middleton deliured to his matie ye 25 of Maye 1606.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Alre</span><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-075-2" href="#sec-04-fn-075" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-075" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> geven from yo<sup>r</sup> freinde the kinge of Bantam sent vnto the kinge of England of Scotland ffraunce &amp; Ireland desiering god to p<sup>r</sup>serue yo<sup>r</sup> health &amp; to exalt yo<sup>u</sup> more &amp; more &amp; all yo<sup>r</sup> Councells. And whereas yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> hath sent Generall Henrie Middleton he came vnto me in health. I did heare that yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> was come to the Crowne of England w<sup>ch</sup> doth greatlie reioyce my harte now England and Bantam are both as one. I haue alsoe receaued a present from yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup>. The w<sup>ch</sup> I giue you many thanke for yo<sup>r</sup> kindnes. I doe send yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> 2 Bezar stones the one waying 14 masses<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-076" href="#sec-04-fn-076" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-076" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> &amp; the other 3. And soe god haue you in his keepeing.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-160"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="160" data-folio="68" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n159_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 68">[p. 68]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">ffrom the kinge of Turnato to his matie sent by Henrie Middleton or genrall directed to o<sup>r</sup> kinges matie wth a p<sup>r</sup>sent &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ed in May 1606.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">The</span><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-077" href="#sec-04-fn-077" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-077" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> kinge of Mollocco to the kinge ma<sup>tie</sup> of England Scotland ffraunce and Ireland sendeth greeting heareing the good reporte of yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> &amp; of the greatnes of yo<sup>r</sup> kingdomes by the greate Captaine ffrancis Drake in the tyme of my ffather w<sup>ch</sup> was aboute 30 yeares past<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-078" href="#sec-04-fn-078" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-078" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> by the w<sup>ch</sup> Capt: my ffather did send a Ringe vnto the Queene of England as a token of Remembrance w<sup>ch</sup> yf the foresaid Drake were liueing he could Certifie yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> of the greate loue and freindshipp on either side he in the behalfe of the Queene my ffather for him &amp; his successo<sup>rs</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> tyme after the depture of the said Capt we haue dalie expected his retourne, my father liueing many yeares after. And I after the death of my ffather liued in the same hope till I was ffather to 11 Children, since w<sup>ch</sup> tyme we haue beene informed that English men were men of bad dispotition, &amp; came not as peaceable m<sup>r</sup>chaunte but as theeues. and robbers and to depose vs of o<sup>r</sup> Countries, w<sup>ch</sup> by the comeing of the bearer hereof yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>ties</sup> servant Capt: Henrie Middleton we haue found to the Contrary whereof we greatelie reioyce. And after many yeares of expectacõn of some English fforces by the pmyse of Capt: Drake heere arriued certeine shipps w<sup>ch</sup> we well hoped had beene English men, but they pued to be Hollenders, &amp; then being out of all hope of sucker of the english nation we were enforced to wryte to the Prince of Holland to craue aide and succo<sup>r</sup> of him agaynst o<sup>r</sup> auncient enymies the Portingalles. And according to o<sup>r</sup> request he hath sent hither his forces w<sup>ch</sup> hath expelled all the Portingalls out of the fforte they here hould att Ambweno<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-079" href="#sec-04-fn-079" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-079" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> and Tydore, And whereas yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup> hath sent vnto vs a most kinde łre by Capt Henrie Middleton yt doth not a little reioyce vs: as alsoe the said Capt Henr Middleton was desierous <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-161" data-page="161" data-folio="69" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n160_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 69">[p. 69]</button> to leaue a ffactory here we were very willinge there vnto, and gaue o<sup>r</sup> Consent, w<sup>ch</sup> the Admirall of the Hollenders vnderstandinge he came and Challenged a former pmyse w<sup>ch</sup> we had wrytte to the Prince of Holland that yf he would send vs such Succo<sup>r</sup> as should expell the Portugalls out of theis pt<em>e</em> that noe other Nation should haue trade heare but onelie they, soe that we were enforced agaynst o<sup>r</sup> likeing to yeald vnto the Hollenders Capt request for this p<sup>r</sup>sent tyme. Wherefore we craue pardon of yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup>. And whereas the Hollenders Admirall doth soliscite vs not to hold any ffreindshipp w<sup>th</sup> the englishe nor to giue eare nor Creditt to yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>te</sup> łres: yett neu<sup>r</sup>theless for all their sute yf yt please you to send hither agayne they shall be most freindlie wellcome, &amp; in token of ffreindshipp w<sup>ch</sup> we desire w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> ma<sup>tie</sup>. Wee haue sent you a small remembraunce of a Bahare of Cloues o<sup>r</sup> Countrie being poore &amp; yealding noe other Comoditie w<sup>ch</sup> we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept in good parte. And soe we leaue you to god who bless you and all yo<sup>r</sup> Councells. <em>Tarnata.</em></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Theis 3 foresaid łres were translated out of the India speech into English.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">I MOSTE<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-080" href="#sec-04-fn-080" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-080" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> mightie kinge of Dachem and Sumatra to all psons that shall read this present writeinge, Greeting.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Priuiledges from the kinge of Dachem and Sumatra to the Companie.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">In token</span> of o<sup>r</sup> espetiall freindshipp &amp; vpon many good Consideracõns vs moueing, and cheeflie vppon the Contemplasing of the gratious łres receiued from the most famous Queene of England: we of o<sup>r</sup> espetiall meere motion doe signifie and declare to all people, that we haue intertayned into o<sup>r</sup> freindshipp, <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-162" data-page="162" data-folio="70" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n161_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 70">[p. 70]</button> &amp; holie league o<sup>r</sup> well beloued the <em>SERINISSIMA REGINA DE ENGLA TERRA</em>. to hold and keepe true and faithfull league w<sup>th</sup> her accordinge to the Comendable Course &amp; law of all nations, vnto whose subiecte, we wish much felicitie and therefore doe giue and graunte by theis p<sup>r</sup>sente for vs o<sup>r</sup> heires &amp; successor<sup>rs</sup> as much as in vs lieth to the said subiecte of the most noble Queene of England o<sup>r</sup> Confederate &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y of them theis articles, graunte &amp; priuiledge heereafter expressed and declared.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">FFIRSTE we doe giue and graunte ffree licence aucthoritie &amp; power to all the people, ffactor<sup>s</sup>, m<sup>r</sup>chaunte or subiecte of the Queene of England that they may att all tymes heereafter for eu<sup>r</sup> suerlie and saffelie come into any portes or els wheare into theis o<sup>r</sup> dominiones &amp; kingdome of Sumatra w<sup>th</sup> their shipps goode &amp; m<sup>r</sup>chandize w<sup>th</sup>out any deteinm<sup>te</sup> or hindraunce to them, or to their goode: &amp; theare to abide soiourne, buie &amp; sell, barter and exchaunge according to their owne<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-081" href="#sec-04-fn-081" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-081" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> manner and fashion w<sup>th</sup> all manner of Nations whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> as well as w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> owne naturall subiecte: both in spices &amp; all other m<sup>r</sup>chandizes &amp; soe they may tarrie in o<sup>r</sup> Countrie soe long as they will, &amp; goe away when the list, w<sup>th</sup>out any ympedim<sup>t</sup> lett or hindraunce, paying all such debte as they shall owe to any of o<sup>r</sup> subiecte w<sup>th</sup>in o<sup>r</sup> said dominion.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">FFURTHERMORE o<sup>r</sup> will &amp; pleasure is that all such goode and m<sup>r</sup>chandize as any of the subiecte of the Queene of England shall bringe into this o<sup>r</sup> kingdome or any the partes thereof shall be ffree of all Custome or paym<sup>t</sup> whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> as well for that w<sup>ch</sup> shall be brought into theis o<sup>r</sup> dominions as for all manner of m<sup>r</sup>chandize they shall transporte out of the same for any forreine pte whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ITEM yf any of their shipps in tempest of weather shall be in daunger to be lost, &amp; pishe, &amp; therevpon shall stande in neede of o<sup>r</sup> helpe wee will and Comaund that o<sup>r</sup> vessells w<sup>th</sup> all speede doe helpe &amp; succo<sup>r</sup> them, to saue the same shipps &amp; goode and what may be saued, to retourne to the said m<sup>r</sup>chaunte or their assignes they paying resonable Considerac̃on for their travell.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ITEM yf any Englishe man shall make his will &amp; Testam<sup>te</sup> to <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-163" data-page="163" data-folio="71" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n162_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 71">[p. 71]</button> whomesoeu<sup>r</sup> (by the same will [ )] he shall giue his goode, the ptie shall haue them accordinglie, and yf he die intestate, he to whome the cheefe ffacto<sup>r</sup> or gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> shall saie the goode of the dead to belonge, he shall posses the same.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ITEM that eu<sup>r</sup>y b̄gaine made by the said m<sup>r</sup>chauntę w<sup>th</sup> any manner of p̄son for any kinde of m<sup>r</sup>chandize shall be firme and staple &amp; that noe man of either ptie may shrinke or goe backe from the same &amp; yt shall be lawfull for them to goe before any of o<sup>r</sup> Iustices &amp; to register the same in a Booke according to the teno<sup>r</sup> thereof, yf any Controu<sup>r</sup>sie shall aryse present iustice to be done to both p̄ties, &amp; when they haue sold any of their wares to o<sup>r</sup> subjectę or any other they shall not retourne the said wares vpon the m<sup>r</sup>chauntę hande, but paie for them or ells they shall haue present iustice</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ITEM yf any English m<sup>r</sup>chaunte ffacto<sup>r</sup> or servant shall offend the Agent or cheefe Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of the ffactorie yt shall be lawfull for the said Gouerno<sup>r</sup> to doe iustice vpon the said ptie, or to send him home into England att his pleasuer, &amp; further we graunte vnto the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> power and aucthoritie to end any Controu<sup>r</sup>sie that shall aryse betweene them &amp; to doe iustice accordinge to their owne lawes and Customes.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ITEM we doe graunte the said marchauntę and to their Successo<sup>rs</sup>, that yf any of them or of their servantę shall be wounded or be slaine in any pte or place of o<sup>r</sup> dominion, then informačon thereof being geven we o<sup>r</sup> iustices and other Officers will execute due correcčon &amp; punishm<sup>t</sup> w<sup>th</sup>out delaie accordinge to the cause of the offence, soe that yt shall be an example to all others not to offend in the like. And yf it shall happen the ffactors servauntę or mynisters of the said m<sup>r</sup>chauntę to trespasse or offend, whereby they or any of them shall incurr the daunger of death, or of punishm<sup>t</sup>, that then the goode wares or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes of their Senio<sup>rs</sup> shall not therefore be forfected Confiscate seazed vpon nor spoyled by vs o<sup>r</sup> heires or Successo<sup>rs</sup> or by any of o<sup>r</sup> Officers ministers or subiectę but shall remaine franke &amp; free as dischardged of all punishm<sup>t</sup> &amp; losse.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ITEM we doe p̄myse the foresaid m<sup>r</sup>chauntę graunting yt for eu<sup>r</sup> for vs &amp; o<sup>r</sup> heires that yf any shall bringe any manner of goode or m<sup>r</sup>chandize meete for Treasurie that we will not cause anie <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-164" data-page="164" data-folio="72" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n163_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 72">[p. 72]</button> staie or arrest to be made of their said goode or m<sup>r</sup>chaundize to vs or for o<sup>r</sup> vses w<sup>th</sup>out present Content to be made by o<sup>r</sup> Thr̄er at such reasonable prises as the m<sup>r</sup>chaunte could sell them for ready money, &amp; that noe price or valuacõn shalbe set vpon their goode or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes by vs or any Officers of o<sup>rs</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ITEM we of o<sup>r</sup> goodnes haue graunted to the said English m<sup>r</sup>chaunte their successo<sup>rs</sup> servaunte &amp; deputies that doe or shall remayne in any pte of this o<sup>r</sup> kingdome of Sumatra whether yt be in Dachem or any other pte of o<sup>r</sup> dominion freelie to keepe their owne lawe &amp; in any wyse none of o<sup>rs</sup> to force them to o<sup>r</sup> lawe or faith against their wills.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ITEM yf any man shall saie, that theis being Christians haue spoken any thinge to the derogacõn of o<sup>r</sup> holie faith &amp; religion &amp; haue slaundered the same, in this matter as well as in all others there shall noe false witnes in any case be admitted.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">LAST of all to thintent that this league w<sup>th</sup> her ma<sup>tie</sup> &amp; the aforesaid graunte &amp; priuiledge w<sup>th</sup> her subiecte, may the better be obserued and inuiolablie be pformed, we by the grace of God greate kinge of Dachem &amp; Sumatra for vs o<sup>r</sup> heires &amp; sucssessors doe pmyse by o<sup>r</sup> princelie word in stead of an Oath inviolablie to maintaine &amp; preserue &amp; will cause to be inuiolablie kept p<sup>r</sup>served &amp; mayntayned frome tyme to tyme &amp; att all tymes heereafter &amp; for the more ratificacõn we comaund all o<sup>r</sup> Captaines Iudges Customers Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>rs</sup> &amp; servaunte wheresoeu<sup>r</sup> theis łres or priuiledge shall be seene throughout all o<sup>r</sup> dominions that they be obeyed in all pointe accordinglie as longe as the Queene of England of her pte shall duelie keepe and obserue this league &amp; holie peace expressed in this priuiledge w<sup>th</sup> vs and o<sup>r</sup> subiecte we alsoe of o<sup>r</sup> Royall pte doe chardge &amp; Comaund the same soe longe to be strayghtlie kept &amp; obserued. And the Contrarie to this diuyne league and priuiledge, lett noe man presume to doe or saie any thinge.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-165"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="165" data-folio="73" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n164_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 73">[p. 73]</button></div><h2 class="sec-h1">A DISCRIPCON<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-082" href="#sec-04-fn-082" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-082" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> of the Iland of Banda w<sup>th</sup> the Townes and Ilande thereof: w<sup>th</sup> the wares or marchandizes therein spent &amp; vttered &amp; wares to be bought theare as followeth.</h2><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">TO witt Lunter<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-083" href="#sec-04-fn-083" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-083" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Nere<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-084" href="#sec-04-fn-084" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-084" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> in w<sup>ch</sup> towne we bought o<sup>r</sup> Nutmegges &amp; maces, and theare sould diuers sortes of o<sup>r</sup> Stuffe Pinthados Iava girdles in barter for maces &amp; nutte<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-085" href="#sec-04-fn-085" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-085" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> Madgawgen, Lykou, Artatten,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-086" href="#sec-04-fn-086" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-086" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> Semar, Kamber<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-086-2" href="#sec-04-fn-086" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-086" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> Rere, Anwen, Dunno<sup>r</sup>, Dedyer, Rossengen,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-087" href="#sec-04-fn-087" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-087" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> Laba taker<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-086-3" href="#sec-04-fn-086" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-086" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> Latter, Loekra Regacke Rata muferr Polayu,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-088" href="#sec-04-fn-088" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-088" role="doc-noteref">7</a></sup> Palroyen.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-089" href="#sec-04-fn-089" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-089" role="doc-noteref">8</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">India wares</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Townes and places where the are made.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Wares and m<sup>r</sup>chandizes spent &amp; vttered there namelie stuffe or Clothes as our men call them: &amp; called by the Dutchmen kleetghees<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-090" href="#sec-04-fn-090" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-090" role="doc-noteref">9</a></sup> being the same &amp; such like stuffe as S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster tooke w<sup>ch</sup> are made att Bengalla Mesepatamya<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-091" href="#sec-04-fn-091" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-091" role="doc-noteref">10</a></sup> Cheremandalle &amp; St Thome, and as some saye alsoe att Suratt &amp; Cambaya w<sup>ch</sup> stuffes as the dutchmen saie are thus called viz Dragon, Patta Kassa of the ffinest <em>Torya, Baffa, Telle Pricken, Kantkey</em>, <em>Mossaffey, Patta Malim Tzijnd, Sorassa Madynceus</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-092" href="#sec-04-fn-092" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-092" role="doc-noteref">11</a></sup> Brasse peeces of Ordinances w<sup>th</sup> Chambers in the breeches w<sup>ch</sup> are to be had in Bantam but brought from Chyna as thought. All theis aboue said wares are well vttered in those Townes.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><em>Rise and Sagur</em> w<sup>ch</sup> is at the barke of a Tree, or made of a Tree w<sup>ch</sup> they buie &amp; eate insteed of bread.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Rise and Sagur is their bread.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">English wares.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Nowe followeth English Comodities to be <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-166" data-page="166" data-folio="74" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n165_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 74">[p. 74]</button> theare sold Broad sword blades Royalls of 8. English Clothes Reds &amp; stamels<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-093" href="#sec-04-fn-093" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-093" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> of 10, 12 or 14<sup>s</sup> ste the yarde veluette Redd velvette greene branched w<sup>th</sup> fflowers &amp;c: w<sup>ch</sup> are not English but China veluette &amp; to be had att Bantam of the Chineses much better cheepe then we can carrie English veluette thither.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Spices.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">ffor all the foresaid wares &amp; Royalls the people of <em>Banda, Turnato, Tedora</em> &amp; other places of the <em>Molloccos</em> doe giue in Barter mace Nutmegge &amp; Cloues.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Maces gathered 3 tymes a yeare.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">In the places &amp; Townes abouesaid maces are gathered 3 tymes in a yeare viz in Iulie is the greatest, continuing vntill September. Againe in December &amp; againe in ffebruarie. The last twoe gatheringe are but small in quantitie, the greate gatheringe the call their <em>Payty</em>, &amp; the 2 small gatheringe, the call <em>Ara</em> and <em>Messon Iaua</em>.</p></div><h3 class="sec-h2">A DISCRIPTION of the Iland of Amboyna translated out of Dutch 1605.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-094" href="#sec-04-fn-094" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-094" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></h3><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Cloues heare but euery 3 yeare.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Indie wares.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">HEARE groweth aboundance of Cloues not eu<sup>r</sup>y yeare, but eu<sup>r</sup>y third yeare &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y third yeare is a greate growth now this present yeare of <em>Anno dñi</em> 1605. Heare are diuers sortes of East Indy <em>wares</em> uttered as followeth <em>Clothes</em> as o<sup>r</sup> men vse to call them, &amp; called by the dutchmen <em>Kletgees</em> being linnen &amp; made of Cotten woll, or the same stuffe, that <em>Callicutt cloth</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-095" href="#sec-04-fn-095" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-095" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> is made, to cast aboute their bodies as <em>Clokes</em> or <em>Mantles</em>, or as girdles and <em>Skarffes</em> aboute their loynes: named as followeth &amp; for w<sup>ch</sup> the <em>Amboyneses</em> doe giue vs Cloues in barter. <em>Tooria, black Baffatta</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-096" href="#sec-04-fn-096" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-096" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> <em>Keykam, Sallallo, Pattala</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-097" href="#sec-04-fn-097" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-097" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> <em>Sarassa</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-098" href="#sec-04-fn-098" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-098" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-167" data-page="167" data-folio="75" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n166_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 75">[p. 75]</button> <em>Tzier</em>, <em>Mallya</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-099" href="#sec-04-fn-099" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-099" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> <em>Patta</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-100" href="#sec-04-fn-100" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-100" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> <em>Mora</em>, <em>Tanknyla</em>, there<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-101" href="#sec-04-fn-101" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-101" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> are white Clothes w<sup>th</sup> Red stripes on the end <em>blew kanteky black Mossaffey</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-102" href="#sec-04-fn-102" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-102" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> <em>blew Cheney</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-103" href="#sec-04-fn-103" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-103" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> Clothes all or the most pte of theis abouesaid are made aboute <em>Bengalla</em>, <em>Mesapatamya St Thome Cheremandall</em> or <em>Cambaya</em> &amp; some other pte of the East Indies.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-168"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="168" data-folio="76" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n167_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 76">[p. 76]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">English or Hollandes wares.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Now follow the English or Hollande wares theare vendible some head peeces some graven some guilt, long peeces w<sup>th</sup> smap hances,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-104" href="#sec-04-fn-104" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-104" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> the cocke to stand on the hither side the panne longe stocke &amp; straight y<sup>e</sup> barrell square velvette red, velvette grene, Damaskes, yet not many theis are to be bought in Bantam of the Chyneses &amp; to be carried to the Molloccos.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Painted drinking glasses, lookeinge glasses 3 . 4 . 5 . 6 pipkans<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-105" href="#sec-04-fn-105" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-105" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> being as I thinke small pewter or woodden kanns made w<sup>th</sup> spowte to drinke in w<sup>ch</sup> are made cheeflie in Holland as I thinke some kniues I thinke sleight kniues, some w<sup>th</sup> painted haftes<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-106" href="#sec-04-fn-106" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-106" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> of wood or boane.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Royalls of 8 <em>Tenkees</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-107" href="#sec-04-fn-107" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-107" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> w<sup>ch</sup> I suppose are <em>Ellaphaunts</em> teeth.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">deceipt in Cloues.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">most store of cloues</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Theis people are deceiptefull in their Cloues men must take heede least they putt dust to make them heauie or mingle some such stuffe amonge them w<sup>ch</sup> you must carefullie finde &amp; spie out in tyme, Theis be the names of the Townes in <em>Amboyna Hito</em>, <em>Mamelen</em>, <em>Nusitelle</em> &amp; the <em>Castle</em>, but at <em>Amboyna</em> we shall not get any <em>Cloues</em> because of the <em>Hollenders</em>. Theis townes lie vpon one Iland viz: <em>Lowe Lossiury</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-108" href="#sec-04-fn-108" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-108" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> <em>Cambella</em>, <em>Keyebabbit Lackey Latu</em>. But att lowe <em>Cambella</em> &amp; <em>Hito</em> falleth most cloues.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">the best roade for ships: good people.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">And is alsoe the best Roade for shipps &amp; are a good people.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-169"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="169" data-folio="77" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n168_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 77">[p. 77]</button></div><h3 class="sec-h2">A DISCRIPTION of the Iland <em>Selebes</em> or <em>Makasser</em>.</h3><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Selebes.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">India wares</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">China wares</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">English wares</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">thearein</span> are spent &amp; vttered theis wares followinge Clothes as we call them, and by the Hollenders called <em>kletgees, Towit dragon, Patta, Mea, Torya, Baffa, knitt Sipann, Threle</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-109" href="#sec-04-fn-109" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-109" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> <em>Ormesines</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-110" href="#sec-04-fn-110" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-110" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> or Taffeties Read velvet braunched w<sup>ch</sup> Ormysenes &amp; velvette are to be bought att Bantam of the Chineses much better cheepe then English velvette. Read or stamells English Cloth as aboue aboute 10. 12 or 14<sup>s</sup> star the yarde, Royalls of 8, for all w<sup>ch</sup> we shall buie Ryse, Indico Lapes Bezar w<sup>ch</sup> theare in aboundance are to be hadd.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Rise good wares to buye heare.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">The Rice you may carry from hence to <em>Amboyna and Banda</em> where you may gett for yt any sorte of spices before all wares that can be carried thither, or for w<sup>ch</sup> you may gett any lading theare.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Heathen but a good people &amp; good iustice.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Glasses kniues.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">The are heathen people but verie good people to deale w<sup>th</sup>all &amp; to liue by: and w<sup>ch</sup> doe hold good right &amp; iustice &amp; order after their manner Glasses to drinke in, lookeing glasses &amp; kniues are there well requested.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A discripcon of the Iland of Botton. slaues.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">China dishes.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">India clothes.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">veluetts and Taffeties.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">in this</span> Iland of Botonar<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-111" href="#sec-04-fn-111" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-111" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> are some good <em>slaues</em> but noe good of their owne but what are thither brought from Iava for <em>Slaues</em> for heare the buie and sell <em>Slaues</em> as a good m<sup>r</sup>chandize <em>China dishes</em> or <em>Puselen</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-112" href="#sec-04-fn-112" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-112" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> of all sorte of the finest <em>Clothes</em> I meane India Cloth [es c]alled Tenexto<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-113" href="#sec-04-fn-113" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-113" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> or Tannies<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-114" href="#sec-04-fn-114" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-114" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> are heare well requested. <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-170" data-page="170" data-folio="78" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n169_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 78">[p. 78]</button> Clothes, english Clothes, Read, stamels, velvette of all manner of sorte &amp; worke of the finest Damaskes &amp; Taffeties, (not english silke) well requested. Theis Damaske &amp; Taffeties as some men saie are alsoe made att Damaske &amp; carried to Bantam, &amp; soe to the Moloccos</p></div><h3 class="sec-h2">A DISCRIPTION of the Iland of Seran<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-115" href="#sec-04-fn-115" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-115" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></h3><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Bras peeces.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">longe peces &amp; head peces &amp; harnest.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">IN THIS Iland of Seran falleth noe other wares then Segu,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-116" href="#sec-04-fn-116" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-116" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> w<sup>ch</sup> is of a tree in the Iland of Banda, is well requested, &amp; is their bread &amp; they cannot want<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-117" href="#sec-04-fn-117" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-117" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> yt. Therefore is caried from hence thither and sold to great proffitt, men sell theare theis kinde of Indie Clothes. <em>Madmayrens</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-118" href="#sec-04-fn-118" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-118" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> <em>Sallallo</em>, <em>Kassa</em>, <em>Dragon</em>, <em>Patta</em>, <em>Kantekey</em>, <em>Bassa</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-119" href="#sec-04-fn-119" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-119" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> <em>kreyckam</em> Brasse peeces of Ordinance like bases<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-120" href="#sec-04-fn-120" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-120" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> haueing Chambers in the breeches w<sup>ch</sup> brasse peeces are made in <em>China</em> well formed &amp; are to be bought att Bantam, longe peeces snaphances such as are in the former pte hereof, described head peeces white, graven and guilt &amp; Corslette are here well requested. But note in this Iland there is nothinge to be had for retourne for o<sup>r</sup> Countrie yet store of Sagu for to make bread to be sold to good pfitt onelie att Banda.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">HEARE followeth the Table of the India clothes as we call Clothes named in the former parte hereof and how to know them to witt. Tzinde, are silke Clothes w<sup>th</sup> dead<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-121" href="#sec-04-fn-121" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-121" role="doc-noteref">7</a></sup> stripes. Patta, katnynen w<sup>th</sup> read stripes ou<sup>r</sup>thwarte through. Dragon, blacke and Read. Sallallo, blew &amp; blacke. Basta are white &amp; blacke, starched &amp; foulded vpp fower square.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-171"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="171" data-folio="79" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n170_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 79">[p. 79]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Kassa are white vnstarched lawnes. Kreckam, is Read starched. Kantekey, black starched. Mossaffey is black of the same, starched. Toorya not starched, are painted like as the Sarassa but are verie Course. Borne Laya, are w<sup>th</sup> white &amp; blacke quarles<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-122" href="#sec-04-fn-122" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-122" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> or Checkers like as Poliyng kintsy.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-123" href="#sec-04-fn-123" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-123" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Paw are silke Clothes w<sup>th</sup> freinge<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-124" href="#sec-04-fn-124" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-124" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> att the end w<sup>th</sup> little quarles of checo<sup>r</sup> through.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To be bought in Bantam but made in &amp;c:</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Theis foresaid Clothes as we call them are all tymes well to be had att Bantam, yett att some tymes better then att other some: but are made in Bengalla att Mesapatania<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-125" href="#sec-04-fn-125" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-125" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> S<sup>t</sup> Thome Cheremandalla.</p></div><div class="ledger-table-block" id="tbl-001"><div class="table-toolbar"><span class="table-id-badge">TBL-001 · FOLIO 171</span><span class="table-csv-links"><a href="/downloads/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/datasets/tables/tbl-001_p0171.csv" download>Diplomatic CSV</a> · <a href="/downloads/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/datasets/tidy/tbl-001_p0171.tidy.csv" download>Tidy CSV</a></span></div><div class="ledger-table-wrap" role="region" aria-label="Statistical table" tabindex="0"><table class="ledger-table"><thead><tr><th scope="col"></th><th scope="col">Royalls</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr class="row-data"><td>Dragon y<sup>e</sup> Cody or Cordge</td><td>040 Royals</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Patta the Cordge</td><td>055 Royalls</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Kassa the Corge</td><td>080 Royals</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Tzyle the Corge</td><td>070 Royalls</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Sarassa the Corge</td><td>100 Royalls</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Gobber the Corge</td><td>120 Royals</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Tsidnde the Corge</td><td>140 Royalls</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Kntsy paw<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-126" href="#sec-04-fn-126" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-126" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> the Corge</td><td>093 Royals</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Blatty the Corge</td><td>060 Royals</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Moredie y<sup>e</sup> Cordge or Codie</td><td>030 Royals</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Maduyevs<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-127" href="#sec-04-fn-127" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-127" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> y<sup>e</sup> Corge</td><td class="num">013</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Baffata y<sup>e</sup> Cordge</td><td class="num">100</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Kantekey y<sup>e</sup> Corge</td><td class="num">020</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Mosaffey y<sup>e</sup> Corge</td><td class="num">032</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Toria the Corge</td><td class="num">016</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Kneckam<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-128" href="#sec-04-fn-128" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-128" role="doc-noteref">7</a></sup> the Corge</td><td class="num">025</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Polinge the Corge</td><td class="num">035</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Borne ladye the Corge</td><td class="num">030</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-172"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="172" data-folio="80" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n171_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 80">[p. 80]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Note the Cody or Corge is a bondell or set nomber of 20 peeces.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Theis are the preces that the will yeald at Banda &amp; other places of the Molloccos as this now saith And o<sup>r</sup> ffactor[s] Thomas Browne &amp; Woodnoth who were att the Moloccos cann more better &amp; more fullie enforme you.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">But at the places where theis are made &amp; bought aboue mencioned they are much better Cheepe</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">The waighte of Banda and Amboyna as the dutchman hath written but beleeue yt not w<sup>th</sup>out tryall because yt agreeth not to Anthonie Writes &amp; o<sup>r</sup> other ffactor<sup>s</sup> Accompte, neither did we finde yt to fall out soe, by o<sup>r</sup> last Cloues.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">In Banda 10 Catty<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-129" href="#sec-04-fn-129" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-129" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> is one Inlande or that Countrie Barr or one Barr of that place. 100 Catty one Portugalls Barr maketh Hollande weight 537¾.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-130" href="#sec-04-fn-130" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-130" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">In Amboyna 50 Barratt is on Portuge Barr w<sup>ch</sup> maketh 550<sup>li</sup> Hollande.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">One Amboynes barr 20 Barrette. 1 Barrett 11<sup>li</sup> Hollande this I vnderstand not.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">In Bantam one Sacke of pepp maketh 60<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-131" href="#sec-04-fn-131" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-131" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> Hollende. The weight of Rice is accordinge to the Barr to witt.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">In Banda &amp; Amboyna 6<sup>li</sup> Hollande In Marcassar 7<sup>li</sup> Hollende &amp; 800 maketh 1 } This is the koyem<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-132" href="#sec-04-fn-132" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-132" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> as the Inhabitante there call yt       } weight of Rice.</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3">NOTES<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-133" href="#sec-04-fn-133" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-133" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> BY m<sup>r</sup>. Co:<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-134" href="#sec-04-fn-134" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-134" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> whoe sayled theis waies as he said</h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Note that vpon the sholes of India by S<sup>t</sup> Lawrence when you haue 80. or 90 ffaddam, then looke out for the fflatte<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-135" href="#sec-04-fn-135" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-135" role="doc-noteref">7</a></sup> are neere <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-173" data-page="173" data-folio="81" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n172_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 81">[p. 81]</button> therefore cary but small sayle yf yt be in the night: but yf in the daye you shall see the Sea breake on the Rocke &amp; broken grounde you may Anco<sup>r</sup> in saffetie betweene the twoe Ilande, and the third Iland is the mayne Iland where is noe good thinge but water.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><span class="sc">Mosombecke</span>.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">touchinge the entringe to Mosombeeke compare yt wth Linscot<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-137" href="#sec-04-fn-137" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-137" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> fol: 311 chap: 3</aside></div><p class="prose-p">When you soyle to Monsombeeke<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-136" href="#sec-04-fn-136" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-136" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> you must goe beyond the Roade and come backe againe w<sup>th</sup>in because of the shoels &amp; there you shall ryde in fiue fadam faire ground vnder the Iland right agaynst the Castle, haueing vpon yo<sup>r</sup> starboard the Iland &amp; larboard side the Castle Then you must sayle betweene the shoels and th[e] land to come vnto the Roade of Monsombeeke being there the people of the land &amp; Portugalls will come aboard you.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">When o<sup>r</sup> shipps come (yf yt please God) to <em>Mosombeeke</em> in the Roade there will come abourde the people of the land &amp; Portugalls, the Portugals beleeue not, neither trust the Countrie people too farr, they of the Countrie will bringe you (yf you please) beyond the Castle wheare the Portugall is . then shall you haue the Inhabitante come aboard you w<sup>th</sup> their Comodities followinge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">wares for vs to buie.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">wares for vs to sell.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Sansabar.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">good victuals.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Pember.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Allasackatrinas Ilandes neere the Read Sea: victuals: good Harbor.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Cambaya.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Amber greece Gould Oliphaunte teeth &amp; Indico but that Indico is of no great value but verie Course. The m<sup>r</sup>chandize you carrie thither is blew Callicoes &amp; diu<sup>r</sup>s other wares made aboute Bengalla English wares Tynn, Iron, Steele leade pennie kniues scissors, Rasers, w<sup>ch</sup> must be verie good, longe peece w<sup>th</sup> match, Pistols Rapeires but noe sworde lookeing glasses &amp; beade ffrom Mosombeeke saile vpp to Sanzabar,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-138" href="#sec-04-fn-138" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-138" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> there you may ride in good ground, but verie deepe &amp; halfe a league from the shore but come noe nerer to the Land but 40 fadam for there is fowle ground, you shall haue there, hogge Goate Hemp greate store, Orrenges, Lemans, baffeilde,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-139" href="#sec-04-fn-139" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-139" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> Potato<sup>rs</sup>, Plantanns and Maskinilliacos,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-140" href="#sec-04-fn-140" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-140" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> &amp; all manner of refreshinge <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-174" data-page="174" data-folio="82" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n173_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 82">[p. 82]</button> for aleece of Iron a paire of Sissers, or small triffles you shall haue a buffeild or other good victualls ffrom Sanzabar sayle to Pember<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-141" href="#sec-04-fn-141" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-141" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> leaueing Pemb̃ on the larboard side &amp; soe sailinge 20 or 30 leagues from the shoare all along to the Iland of Allasackatrina<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-142" href="#sec-04-fn-142" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-142" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> w<sup>ch</sup> is neare to the Redd Sea. In w<sup>ch</sup> Iland of Allasakatrina are good harbor<sup>rs</sup> faire depth and good Ancho<sup>r</sup> ground and aboundaunce of good victualls (note they went not into the Read Sea. [)] ffrom <em>Allasackatrina</em> they tooke their Course directlie to Cambaya but that was onelie to take a prize &amp; not for traffique.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Goa.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">ffrom the Coaste of Cambaya the sayled into the riuer Goa they were at &amp; aboute Goa the latter end of October &amp; staied three weekes or amonneth &amp; had faire weather.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">At the entrie of the riuer Goa are 2 Castles verie stronge well appointed w<sup>th</sup> verie greate Ordenaunce the shott bullettę of 50<sup>li</sup> and the people of the Towne of Goa, &amp; all the Countrie 6 leagues aboute are vnder the Gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>te</sup> of the Portugalls &amp; will not by any meanes displease the Portugalls nor traffique w<sup>th</sup> any people but the Portugalls onely, but will rather betraye all men, yea even the Spanniardę in reguard of the Portugals They sayled round aboute the towne &amp; Ilandę of Goa and fought w<sup>th</sup> diuers vessells and ffriggettę soe that in this towne there is noe traffique to be looked for.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Cananor.</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Indie wares</aside></div><p class="prose-p">ffrom <em>Cambaya</em> the sailed all alonge the Coaste w<sup>th</sup>in saker<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-143" href="#sec-04-fn-143" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-143" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> shott of the shoare vntill they came to <em>Cananor</em> they were besett w<sup>th</sup> ffriggettę &amp; gallies of the Portugalls but of noe greate force, but they Inhabitantę of the Countrie did come abourd them eu<sup>r</sup>y daie all alongst the Coast to traffique bringing to them theis Comodities<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-144" href="#sec-04-fn-144" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-144" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup></p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-175"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="175" data-folio="83" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n174_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 83">[p. 83]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">English wares</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">How to trie yor Bezar.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Rialls of 8, pistolls longe peeces, rapyers, but noe sworde. All w<sup>ch</sup> they sell againe to the Portugalls because the Portugalls will buie nothinge of vs Hollenders Take vnsacked lyme &amp; beate yt to powder, &amp; lay yt vpon a lynnen cloth then wett yo<sup>r</sup> Bezar vpon yo<sup>r</sup> tongue, &amp; rub yt vpon the lyme dust yf yt geue any Coullo<sup>r</sup> it is false, but yf yt geue noe Colo<sup>r</sup> it is right &amp; good.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Malabar</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Samarins note that the Hollenders soe call them but not founde in the mapp</aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Indie wares.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">ffrom <em>Cananor</em> the sayled all alonge the Coast of Malabar by the Samarins<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-04-fn-145" href="#sec-04-fn-145" data-fn-target="sec-04-fn-145" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Countrie towarde Callicutt in all w<sup>ch</sup> Coast the people of the Countrie will come abourd &amp; traffique w<sup>th</sup> you bringing theis Comodities ffollowing Viz pearles Rubies Saphers Diamonde &amp; many other good thinge but in any hand goe not ashoare to trust them.
